Therefore indeed, servant of God, I receive without excuse thy injunction, which bids me demonstrate more from nature, without the testimony of Scripture and of the Fathers, whether the soul is an incorporeal creature; taking courage once more from the drops of your teaching, with which thou hast bedewed me in various ways when I was parched with ignorance.

But who are these that affirm there to be no incorporeal creature? And with what non-existent fabrications are these opposing the truths of your words? And how, unless they are more contentious than is fitting, were they not put out of countenance by your argument, which stands so very far from every opposing account? And so that my account of this may proceed rather in due order, I shall make your wise argument itself, as an unconquerable ally of the truth, the first-fruits of my own; priding myself on what is yours as provisions for the road.

1. If body is by its own nature unmoved, and the soul too is body, even if the most fine-grained, as they say, then insofar as it is body it will not be moved. And if this is so, whence have we movement? And so on to infinity, until we come to an incorporeal creature. But if they say that we are moved by God in the first account, then — since I know most of our movements to be out of place and shameful — they will of necessity declare the Divine the cause of these too.

2. Another. And again: if every bodily existence that is in any way at all, according to the true account, being bounded by quantity and quality, in bulk and form, and surface and figure, sets the limit of the contemplation of itself, as being bounded by necessary limits — for he who searches it out can imagine nothing outside these — and the soul too is body, then it is altogether circumscribed either as from these, or as these, or as some of them, or as some one of these, or by these, or by some, or by some one of these. But in fact it is circumscribed neither as from these, nor as these, nor as some, nor as some one of these, nor by these, nor by some, nor by some one of these. Therefore the soul is not body.

3. Another.

ἡ ψυχή· οὖσα δὲ οὐδὲν ὑπάρχει ὧν καταλαμβάνει ἡ ἔννοια, ἐξ ὧν, ἢ ἃ ὑπάρχει τὰ σώματα· οὐ χρῶμα, οὐ σχῆμα, οὐχ ἀντιτυπία, οὐ βάρος, οὐ πηλικότης, οὐχ ἡ εἰς τρία διάστασις· καὶ ἁπλῶς ὅλως οὐδὲν οὖσα τῶν περὶ τὴν σωματικὴν φύσιν καταλαμβανομένων· ἢ μηδὲν οὖσα τούτων, ὡς ἔδειξεν ὁ λόγος, ἀσώματος ἔσται πάντως, εἴπερ ἐστίν.

428δʹ. Ἄλλο. Ἔτι, εἰ πᾶσα συστολὴ καὶ διαστολὴ καὶ τομὴ τῶν σωμάτων ἐστί· τὸ δὲ πάσης συστολῆς καὶ διαστολῆς καὶ τῆς οἱασοῦν τομῆς ἐλεύθερον, πάντως ἀσώματον, ἡ ψυχὴ ἄρα ἀσώματος, ὡς τούτων πάντων Τὸ, πάντων, plerisque deest. παντελῶς ἀνεπίδεκτος.

εʹ. Ἄλλο. Εἰ πάσης τῆς σωματικῆς οὐσίας, εἰς τε τὸ ἔμψυχον καὶ τὸ ἄψυχον διῃρημένης· σῶμα δὲ καὶ ἡ ψυχὴ, ἢ ἔμψυχον, ἢ ἄψυχον ἔσται πάντως. Ἀλλ᾿ εἰ μὲν ἔμψυχον, διὰ ψυχούσης πάντως, ἢ οὐσίας, ἢ δυνάμεως, ἤτοι συμβεβηκότος ψυχωθήσεται. Ψυχὴν δὲ ψυχοῦσθαι λέγειν, ὥσπερ καὶ φῶς φωτίζεσθαι, ἢ θερμαίνεσθαι τὸ πῦρ, παντελῶς καταγέλαστον. Καὶ εἰ μὲν οὐσίαν ψυχοῦν τὴν ψυχὴν εἴπωμεν, ἢ σῶμα ἢ ἀσώματον αὐτὴν πάλιν λέξομεν. Καὶ εἰ μὲν σῶμα, τὰς αὐτὰς ἀεὶ τῶν λογισμῶν ἀτόπους ἐξ ἀτόπων ἐφόδους, ὁ περὶ τούτου δέξεται λόγος, μέχρις οὗ ἀσώματον τὴν ψυχὴν εἶναι συνδιομολογηθῇ. Εἰ δὲ δύναμιν, ἤτοι ποιότητα ψυχοῦν τὴν ψυχὴν εἴπωμεν, τῆς οὐσίας ζωοποιητικόν τε καὶ κινητικὸν, τὸ ἀκούσιον ἔσται καὶ ἀνυπόστατον. Πρὸς τὸ , καὶ ἐναλλὰξ καὶ παρὰ φύσιν τὴν κατὰ τὸ Alii codd. τῷ, quod melius. ἄρχειν καὶ ἄρχεσθαι εἶναι διαφοράν· εἴπερ σώματος ἄρχειν κατὰ φύσιν δημιουργηθεῖσα ἡ ψυχὴ, ὡς ἀσώματος οὐσία, ὡς σῶμα κατ᾿ αὐτοὺς ὑπὸ τοῦ μὴ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν ὄντος· τῆς κατὰ τὸ ποιὸν λέγω δυνάμεως, ἀρχθήσεται· ἡ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν οὖσά τε καὶ ὑφεστῶσα, καὶ τὴν κοινὴν πάντων πρόληψιν. Εἰ δὲ ἄψυχον αὐτὴν εἴπωμεν σῶμα, ἀναίσθητον καὶ ἀφαντασίαστον, ἄλογόν τε καὶ ἀδιανόητον ἔσται. Ἀλλὰ μὴν ταῦτα πάντα περὶ τὴν ψυχὴν, καὶ ἐν τῇ ψυχῇ, καὶ ἔστι καὶ ὁρᾶται· οὐκ ἄρα σῶμα ἡ ψυχή. Σῶμα μὲν γὰρ ἔχειν τοῦτο αὐτὴν ἑτεροούσιον, καλῶς εἰδότες λέγειν οὐ παραιτούμεθα· σῶμα δὲ αὐτὴν εἶναι ἀποφαίνεσθαι, ὡς θρασύτατόν τε ἅμα καὶ ἀπαιδευτότατον, παντελῶς ἀποφεύγομεν· πολὺ διαφέρειν τοῦ ἔχειν τὸ εἶναι οὐκ ἀγνοοῦντες. Εἰ δὲ ὅτι ἀσώματον λέγουσι τὸ Θεῖον, τὸν μὴ ὄντα φοβούμενοι φόβον, ἀσώματον εἰπεῖν τὴν ψυχὴν οὐ καταδέχονται, ἵνα μὴ εἰς ἴσον αὐτὴν τῷ Θεῷ ἐνέγκωσι· καὶ τὰ λοιπὰ ἡμῶν περικοπτέτωσαν , ἵν᾿ ᾖ αὐτοῖς τῆς ἀπονοίας τέλειον τὸ ἀγώνισμα, ὅσα ἐκ Θεοῦ ὡς μετέχοντες· ἢ Θεὸς ἐξ ἡμῶν, ὡς ἐνεργῶν καὶ διδοὺς, ἢ μεταδιδοὺς, προσηγορεύθημεν, ἢ παρ᾿ ἡμῶν προσηγορεύθη Θεός. Τίνα δὴ λέγω ταῦτα; Τὸ εἶναι, τὸ ζῆν, τὸ φωτίζεσθαι, τὸ ἀγαθύνεσθαι, τὸ λογίζεσθαι, τὸ νοεῖν· τὰ μὲν, διὰ τὴν οὐσίαν· τὰ δὲ, διὰ τὴν κίνησιν, ἤτοι γνώμην καὶ διάθεσιν. Ὄντες γὰρ λεγόμεθα, καὶ ζῶντες, καὶ φῶτες, καὶ ἀγαθοὶ, καὶ νοεροὶ, καὶ λογικοί.

If, then, it is not doubted by them that the soul is; and, being, it is none of the things the mind grasps, out of which, or which, bodies are — not colour, not figure, not resistance, not weight, not magnitude, not extension in three dimensions; and, simply, it is altogether none of the things grasped in bodily nature: inasmuch as it is none of these, as the argument has shown, it will be altogether incorporeal, if indeed it is.

4284. Another. Further: if every contraction and expansion and cut belongs to bodies, and what is free of all contraction and expansion and of any cut whatever is altogether incorporeal, then the soul is incorporeal, as being wholly unreceptive of all these.

5. Another. If, all bodily essence being divided into the animate and the inanimate, the soul too is body, it will altogether be either animate or inanimate. But if animate, it will be ensouled altogether by something that ensouls it — either an essence, or a power, that is, an accident. But to say that a soul is ensouled, as also that light is lit, or that fire is heated, is wholly laughable. And if we say that an essence ensouls the soul, we shall again call that either body or incorporeal. And if body, the account of it will meet always the same absurd approaches of thoughts, out of absurd ones, until it is agreed together that the soul is incorporeal. But if we say that a power, that is a quality, ensouls the soul, then what is without essence and without hypostasis will be that which gives life and movement to the essence. Besides this, the difference that consists in ruling and being ruled will be reversed, and contrary to nature; if indeed the soul, fashioned by nature to rule the body as an incorporeal essence, will — being body according to them — be ruled by what does not exist according to essence, I mean the power according to quality: she who exists and subsists according to essence, as is the common preconception of all. But if we call it inanimate body, it will be without sense and without imagination, irrational and without understanding. But in fact all these both are about the soul and in the soul, and are seen there. Therefore the soul is not body. For that it has this body, which is of another essence, we who know well do not decline to say; but to declare that it is body we altogether shun, as most rash and at the same time most uneducated, not being ignorant that being differs greatly from having.

But if, because they call the Divine incorporeal, they will not admit to call the soul incorporeal, fearing a fear where there is none, lest they bring it to equality with God, then let them cut away the rest of ours as well, so that their contest of folly may be complete: all the names that we have been called from God, as participating; or that God has been called from us, as operating and giving or imparting — or rather, was called God from us. What, then, do I mean by these? Being, living, being illumined, being made good, reasoning, understanding: the ones on account of the essence; the others on account of the movement, that is, inclination and disposition. For we are called beings, and living, and lights, and good, and intellectual, and rational.

Τόν τε Θεὸν ὁμοίως, ὄντα καὶ ζῶντα, καὶ φῶς, καὶ ἀγαθὸν, καὶ νοῦν, καὶ λόγον ὀνομάζομεν· εἴτε ὡς ἐξ αἰτίου ὄντες ταῦτα 429καὶ καλούμενοι· εἴτε ὡς ἐνεργούμενοι, ἐκ τῶν ἡμετέρων ταῦτα τὸ Θεῖον προσαγορεύοντες. Τίς οὖν ὁ λόγος αὐτοῖς, ταῦτα μὲν πάντα πρὸς ἑτέροις πολλοῖς εἶναι τὴν ψυχὴν καὶ ὀνομάζεσθαι· ἀσώματον δὲ, μηκέτι, ὅτιπερ τὸ Θεῖον; Ὡς δέον, ἢ μὴ λέγεσθαι ἡμᾶς ταῦτα, διότι τὸ Θεῖον, ἢ τὸν Θεὸν ταῦτα μὴ ὀνομάζεσθαι, ὅτιπερ καὶ ἡμεῖς. Ἀλλὰ μὴν καὶ ἐσμὲν ταῦτα· καὶ ὀνομαζόμεθα· οὐχ ὡς ταυτὸν τῷ Θεῷ διὰ ταῦτα ὄντες· ἀλλ᾿ ὡς πρὸς αἴτιον αἰτιατὸν, ἢ μέτοχον πρὸς μετεχόμενον, ἢ ὕπαρξιν πρὸς αἰτίαν. Οὐδὲ συγκριτικῶς ἢ συνωνύμως, ὡς ἐπὶ τῶν ὁμοουσίων· ἀλλ᾿ ὁμωνύμως, καὶ οἷον εἰπεῖν μεθεκτῶς· τῶν πραγμάτων κατὰ τὸ ἄπειρον ἀλλήλων τῇ φύσει διεστηκότων· εἰ μὴ τῷ φίλον, ὡς ἀληθῶς, τὸ Θεῖον εἰπεῖν, καὶ ὑπὲρ φύσιν καὶ πρᾶγμα. Ποῦ δὲ λοιπὸν κατ᾿ αὐτοὺς πάλιν, εἰ δοθείη τὴν ψυχὴν εἶναι σῶμα, ὁ τοῦ κατ᾿ εἰκόνα ἡμῖν διασωθήσεται λόγος, εἰ μὴ διὰ πάντων ἔχειν φαμὲν τὴν ψυχὴν, τὴν πρὸς τὸ ἀρχέτυπον ὁμοιότητα; Ὥσπερ γὰρ τοῦ νοεροῦ νοερὰν, καὶ τοῦ ἀθανάτου, ἀφθάρτου τε καὶ ἀοράτου, τὴν ἐν τούτοις γνωριζομένην εἰκόνα ὀνομάζομεν· οὕτω δὴ καὶ τοῦ ἀσωμάτου, ἀσώματον λέγοντες, ὡς πάντα τε ὄγκον, καὶ πᾶσαν ὁμοίως διαφεύγουσαν διαστηματικὴν καταμέτρησιν· ἄλλο τι πάλιν αὐτὴν παρ᾿ ἐκεῖνο, κατὰ τὴν τῆς φύσεως ἰδιότητα λέγομεν. Ἐπεὶ οὐκέτι ἂν εἴη εἰκών, ἀλλ᾿ ἀπαράλλακτος ταυτότης. Ἐν οἷς δὲ ἐν τῇ ἀκτίστῳ καὶ ἀτρέπτῳ καὶ ἀνάρχῳ φύσει ἐκεῖνο καθορᾶται, ἐν τοῖς αὐτοῖς τὴν κτιστὴν λογικὴν φύσιν· οὐ κατὰ τὸ αὐτὸ δὲ, ἀλλ᾿ ὡς ἐν ἐσχάτοις ἀπηχήμασι φωνῆς λόγου δήλωσιν, δείκνυσθαι, ἐναργῶς νομίζομεν. Καὶ ὥσπερ διὰ τῶν ἐν τοῖς οὖσι κατὰ τὴν τοῦ παντὸς διακόσμησιν ἐπιτελουμένων ὁ φιλοσοφώτατος νοῦς, πρὸς τὸ καθαρόν τε καὶ ἄυλον μετενεγκὼν τῶν νοημάτων αὐτοῦ τὰς δυνάμεις, διακρίνει ἀπὸ τῶν κινουμένων τὸ κινοῦν, καὶ ἓν καὶ μόνον καὶ ἁπλοῦν αὐτὸ ἐξ ἑαυτοῦ ὑπάρχον, ὡσαύτως ἔχον καὶ ποιητικὸν αἴτιον καταλαμβάνων ἐν τῇ οἰκείᾳ τῆς ταυτότητος μονιμότητι, ἀπρόσιτον κατὰ τὴν φύσιν τοῖς ἄλλοις ἐπίσταται· ἄτρεπτον μὲν, ὅτι ἀκίνητον· οὐ γάρ ἐστι πρὸς ὃ κινηθήσεται τὸ, τὰ ὄντα καὶ ποιοῦν καὶ πληροῦν, καὶ ὑπὲρ πάντα ὂν τὰ ὄντα· περιεκτικὸν δὲ, ὡς δημιουργὸν, καὶ πρὸ γενέσεως, τῷ ἀπείρῳ τῆς δυνάμεως τὰ πάντα γινῶσκον.

And God likewise we name being, and living, and light, and good, and mind, and reason; either as being these 429and being called these from him as from a cause, or as being operated upon, calling the Divine these from what is ours. What account, then, have they, that the soul both is and is named all these things, besides many others, but no longer incorporeal, because the Divine is? As though it were needful either that we not be called these things because the Divine is, or that God not be named these things because we too are. But in fact we both are these things and are named them — not as being, on account of them, the same as God, but as the caused to the cause, or the participant to the participated, or existence to the cause; nor comparatively or synonymously, as with things consubstantial, but homonymously, and, so to speak, by participation — the things standing apart from one another in nature infinitely; unless it please anyone to say, as is truly so, that the Divine is above both nature and thing.

Where, further, according to them, if it be granted that the soul is body, will the principle of the "according to the image" be preserved for us, unless we say that the soul has the likeness to the archetype through all things? For just as we name the image that is known in these things intellectual, as the image of the intellectual, and so of the immortal, incorruptible and invisible; so too, calling it incorporeal as the image of the incorporeal — as escaping all bulk and likewise all measurement by extension — we yet call it again something other than that, according to the property of nature. For otherwise it would no longer be an image, but an invariant identity. And in the very things in which that is beheld in the uncreated and unchangeable and unoriginate nature, in the same things we clearly hold the created rational nature to be shown — not in the same way, but as the disclosure of a word in the last echoes of a voice.

Κατὰ τὴν αὐτὸν τῆς θεωρίας τρόπον, καὶ διὰ τῆς τῶν μελῶν καὶ μερῶν ποικίλης καὶ ἐντεχνοῦς κινήσεως τοῦ μικροῦ κόσμου Homo μικρὸς κόσμος, non ut apud Greg. μακρός.· λέγω δὴ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου· τὸ κινητικὸν αἴτιον λογιζόμενος, ἄλλο τι οἶδε κατὰ τὴν οὐσίαν παρὰ τὴν ὀργανικὴν τοῦ σώματος ὕπαρξιν· καὶ οὐχ ὡς σῶμα ἐν σώματι· ὅπερ δοκεῖ τοῖς νέοις σοφοῖς, καὶ τῶν ἀναποδείκτων δογματισταῖς· ἀλλ᾿ 432ἁπλοῦν Marg. Fr. βλέπε, vide. καὶ ἐνιαῖον ἐν τοῖς ἐσκεδασμένοις θεωρεῖ, καὶ ἐν τοῖς συνεσταλμένοις, ἀόριστον· τρεπόμενον μὲν, ὡς κινούμενον· κινούμενον δὲ, ὡς ἔχον πρὸς ὃ κινηθήσεται· τῆς δὲ κατὰ τὴν κίνησιν τροπῆς αἴτιον, οὐ τὴν φύσιν γινώσκει, ἀλλὰ τὴν κρίσιν, ὅτ᾿ ἂν ἐσφαλμένη οὖσα τυγχάνῃ αὕτη. Ἡ τροπὴ οὖν, οὐ περὶ αὐτὴν τῆς ψυχῆς ὑπάρχει, ὡς οἶμαι, τὴν οὐσίαν· ἐπεὶ καὶ μετέβαλλεν ἀπειράκις, καὶ οὐχ ἡ αὐτὴ διέμενε κατ᾿ οὐσίαν· ἀλλὰ κατὰ τὴν κίνησιν τὴν ἐφ᾿ ἡμῖν, τῷ αὐτοκρατορικῷ θελήματι συμφερομένη. Τοιγάρτοι τροπῆς δύναμιν ἔχειν τοὺς ἀντιλέγοντας φάσκειν καὶ ἀποφαίνεσθαι, καὶ αὐτὴν τὴν περὶ τὸ Θεῖον τῆς ψυχῆς ἀεικινησίαν, οὐ μὴ δοκεῖ ἀληθευόντων εἶναι· ἀλλὰ πομπικῶς τὸ κομψὸν, καὶ οὐκ εὐλαβῶς τὸ ἀκριβὲς τοῦ λόγου ἐπιζητούντων. Ἐπεὶ τίς ἂν λόγου μέτοχος, καὶ ἀρετῆς φιλίας Alter cod. Reg. καὶ φιλίας. οὐκ ἄμοιρος, ἀγνοεῖ, ὅτι οὐδὲν ὑπάρχει ἕτερον ἡ περὶ τὸ καλὸν τῆς ψυχῆς ἀεικινησία, ἢ ἐνέργεια φυσικὴ, ἐφ᾿ ᾗ καὶ δι᾿ ἣν γεγένηται αἰτίαν τελουμένη; ἡ δὲ τροπὴ, κίνησις παρὰ φύσιν, τὴν ταύτης ἀποτυχίαν τῆς αἰτίας εἰσάγουσα. Τροπὴ γὰρ, κατ᾿ ἐμὲ εἰπεῖν, οὐδὲν καθέστηκεν ἕτερον, ἢ ἀσθένεια, καὶ ἔκπτωσις τῶν κατὰ φύσιν ἐνεργειῶν. Μηκέτι οὖν φᾶναι ἀξίωσον τοῖς λογολέσχαις, Θεὸν καὶ κτίσιν συγκρῖναι τολμήσητε. Ἐπειδὴ τὸ Θεῖον κυρίως εἰπεῖν, οὔτε σῶμα, οὔτε ἀσώματον, οὔτε οὐσία, οὔτε τι τῶν νοουμένων ἢ λεγομένων ἐστὶν, ἵνα μὴ πάντα καθεξῆς ἀπαριθμῶμαι· ἀλλὰ καὶ νοούμενον ἄληπτον, καὶ λεγόμενον ἄρρητον διαμένει. Ταῦτα μὲν οὖν ζητητικῶς, ἀλλ᾿ οὐ δογματικῶς παρ᾿ ἡμῶν εἰρήσθω· καὶ εἰ μὲν αὐτάρκως ἔχει πρὸς τὴν ὑπόθεσιν, ὡς δι᾿ ἐπιτόμου ἐπιστολῆς δυνατὸν, καὶ οὐ πολὺ τῆς ὑμετέρας μεγαλονοίας ἀπολέλειπται· Θεῷ χάρις, καὶ ὑμῖν τοῖς προβιδάζουσιν. Εἰ δὲ βούλεσθε, εἴπερ μὴ πεισθεῖεν οἱ ἀντιλέγοντες, ἀγωνιστικώτερον αὐτοῖς συμπλακῆναι, ἕτοιμοι ἐσμεν Θεοῦ χάριτι, τὸ περικείμενον ἡμῖν τῶν θείων μαρτύρων τε καὶ μαρτυριῶν καταφεῖναι αὐτῶν νέφος· ἵνα πάσης τῆς ἐπιπόνου περὶ τούτου διασκέψεως τὸν ὄγκον ἀποθέμενοι, καὶ τὴν εὐπερίστατον τῶν φιλονείκων λογομαχίαν, ἀνενοχλήτως τὴν ἀληθῆ περὶ τε Θεοῦ καὶ τῶν αὐτοῦ κτισμάτων γνῶσιν κατέχωμεν.

And just as the most philosophic mind, through the things that are accomplished among beings in the ordering of the universe, transferring the powers of its conceptions to the pure and immaterial, distinguishes the mover from the things moved; and, grasping it as one and alone and simple, existing from itself, holding ever the same, and a making cause in the abiding of its own identity, knows it to be by nature unapproachable to the others: unchangeable, because unmoved — for there is nothing toward which it will be moved, that which both makes and fills the things that are, and is above all the things that are — and comprehensive, as Creator, knowing all things even before their coming-to-be by the infinity of its power: in the same mode of contemplation, through the varied and skilful movement of the members and parts of the small world — I mean man — reckoning the moving cause, it knows it to be something other in essence than the organic existence of the body; and not as a body in a body, which is the view of the new wise men and the dogmatists of what is undemonstrated, but 432it contemplates it as simple and unitary in things scattered, and unbounded in things contracted: changeable, as moved; and moved, as having something toward which it will be moved; and of the change in movement it knows the cause to be not the nature, but the judgement, whenever this happens to be mistaken.

Change, then, does not belong, as I think, to the very essence of the soul — for then it would change infinitely often, and would not remain the same according to essence — but to the movement that is in our power, carried along with the sovereign will. Hence for the opponents to assert and declare that even the soul's ever-moving about the Divine has the power of change seems to me to belong not to men who speak the truth, but to men who seek pompously the clever, and not reverently the exactness of the account. For who that shares in reason, and is not without portion in the love of virtue, does not know that the soul's ever-moving about the good is nothing other than natural operation, accomplished toward the cause for which and on account of which it came to be; whereas change is a movement contrary to nature, bringing in the failure of this cause? For change, to speak in my own way, is nothing other than weakness, and a falling away from the operations according to nature. Deign, then, to say to the word-chatterers: No longer dare to compare God and creation. For the Divine, properly speaking, is neither body, nor incorporeal, nor essence, nor any of the things that are understood or spoken — not to enumerate everything in order — but, being understood, it remains ungraspable, and being spoken, ineffable.

Let these things, then, be said by us by way of inquiry and not by way of dogma. And if they are sufficient for the subject, so far as is possible in a short letter, and do not fall much short of your greatness of mind, thanks be to God, and to you who urged me on. But if you wish, in case the opponents are not persuaded, that we grapple with them more combatively, we are ready, by the grace of God, to let loose upon them the cloud of divine witnesses and testimonies that surrounds us; Hebr. xii, 1. so that, laying aside the weight of all laborious inquiry into this, and the easily-besetting word-battle of the contentious, we may hold undisturbed the true knowledge of God and of his creatures.

Εἰ δὲ ἐνδεῶς, ὅπερ πᾶσα παθεῖν ἀνάγκη, καὶ οὐκ ἐπιτετευγμένως, ὡς ἐξ ἀμαθοῦς διανοίας καὶ ἀσθενοῦς ὀργάνου προενηνεγμένα· σύγγνωτέ μοι, ἠγαπημένοι, δι᾿ ὑπακοὴν καὶ μόνην, ὡς ὁ ἐρευνῶν τὰς καρδίας ἐπίσταται Θεὸς, καὶ ταῦτα εἰπεῖν θαρρήσαντι· καὶ διορθώσασθε ἡπίως τὸν οὐκ ἐθέλοντα 433τι τῶν συμφερόντων ἀγνοεῖν, καὶ χειραγωγήσατε συμπαθῶς τὸν ὀρθῶς βαίνειν ἐπιθυμοῦντα.

Ζ΄. — Τοῦ αὐτοῦ πρὸς Ἰωάννην πρεσβύτερον, περὶ τοῦ καὶ μετὰ θάνατον ἔχειν τὴν ψυχὴν τὴν νοερὰν ἐνέργειαν, καὶ μηδεμιᾶς ἐξίστασθαι φυσικῆς δυνάμεως. Τῇ δευτέρᾳ τοῦ ἐνεστῶτος Αὐγούστου μηνὸς τῆς παρούσης πρώτης ἐπινεμήσεως Incidit hæc indictio, juxta Baronii computum, in annum 643, quando Maximus agebat in Africa. Scalig. De emend. temp. et Comput. Maximi apud eundem., ὁ κοινὸς ἡμῶν φίλος τίμια τῆς ὑμετέρας πατρικῆς ἁγιωσύνης γράμματα ἐπιδέδωκέ μοι· οἷς ἐντυχών, παρεκλήθην μὲν εἰκότως ἐφ᾿ ὑμῖν τοῖς ἐμοῖς δεσπόταις, καὶ μετὰ Θεὸν πάντων μοι τῶν καλῶν αἰτίοις, ἐν ὑγείᾳ διάγουσι. Τοσοῦτον γὰρ ὑμῖν ἥνωται δι᾿ ὅλου τὸ Πνεῦμα τοῦ Θεοῦ τὸ ἅγιον, ὅτι καὶ ἀπόντας ὑμᾶς νοὸς ὀφθαλμοῖς μόνον ἀναθεωρῶν, ῥυθμίζομαι κατὰ τὴν ὑπούσάν μοι δύναμιν καὶ βίον καὶ λόγον· ὡς Θεὸν κατὰ τὴν μνήμην δι᾿ ὑμῶν ἐμφανιζόμενον, καὶ ὁρῶν καὶ αἰδούμενος. Ἠνιάθην δὲ οὐ μετρίως ἐπὶ τῷ περὶ ψυχῆς δόγματι τῷ ὑπό τινων, ὡς γεγράφατε, ἀδεῶς ἐκεῖσε κηρυττομένῳ, ὅσην κἀνταῦθα τὸ τοιοῦτο κακὸν ἔχειν ἰσχὺν καὶ παρρησίαν ἐννοῶν. Τὸ δὲ πλέον με κατηφείας ἐμπιπλῶν ἐνταῦθα, καὶ βάρος ὥσπερ νέφος τῇ διανοίᾳ ἐμβάλλον, τὸ νῦν ὑπὸ πάντων σχεδὸν, καὶ μάλιστα τῶν δῆθεν ἐπιφανῶν μοναχῶν πρεσβευόμενον περὶ ἀναστάσεως καινοπρεπὲς δόγμα· ὥστε μηδὲ στοχαστικῶς ἔτι προοιμιάζεσθαι τὴν τοῦ Ἀντιχρίστου παρουσίαν ὑπολαμβάνειν· παρεῖναι δὲ μᾶλλον πιστεύειν καὶ διαβεβαιοῦσθαι, καὶ τὸν ὁπωσοῦν μετέχοντα τοῦ λογίζεσθαι· αὐτὴν μαρτυροῦσαν τῷ τοιούτῳ κακῷ τὴν τῶν λεγομένων [ἀτοπίαν] ἔχοντα.

But if these things have been put forward deficiently — which must quite necessarily have happened — and not successfully, as coming from an unlearned understanding and a weak instrument, forgive me, beloved, who dared to say these things through obedience alone, as God who searches the hearts Psal. vii, 10. knows; and correct gently him who does not wish to be ignorant 433of any of the things that are profitable, and lead by the hand with sympathy him who desires to walk aright.

LETTER 7

Of the same, to John the presbyter, on the soul's having its intellectual operation even after death, and being deprived of no natural power.

On the second of the present month of August, of the present first indiction, our common friend delivered to me the honoured letter of your paternal holiness; on reading which I was comforted, as was fitting, at you, my masters and, after God, the causes of all my good things, being in health. For the Holy Spirit of God has been united with you so wholly that, even beholding you absent with the eyes of the mind alone, I order both life and speech according to the power that is in me; as both seeing and revering God, made manifest in memory through you. But I was grieved not moderately at the doctrine about the soul which, as you have written, is preached there by certain men without fear, considering how much strength and freedom of speech such an evil has here too. And what fills me the more with dejection here, and casts a weight like a cloud upon my understanding, is the new-fangled doctrine about the resurrection now championed by almost everyone, and especially by the supposedly eminent monks; so that even one who shares in reasoning in any way at all no longer supposes by conjecture that the coming of the Antichrist is being prefaced, but rather believes and affirms that it is present, having the very [absurdity] of what is said bearing witness to such an evil.

For they say — they who sharpen their tongue fearlessly against all things, and reckon nothing fearful of what is impiously done or said — that bodies at the resurrection will again be held together for living by phlegm and blood, by yellow and black bile, by the drawing of air and by sensible food; nothing at all new beyond the present life being shown forth through the resurrection, except not being able to die again.

Φασὶ γὰρ οἱ πρὸς πάντα τὴν γλῶσσαν ἀδεῶς ὀξύνοντες, καὶ μηδὲν τῶν ἀσεβῶς γινομένων ἢ λεγομένων ἡγούμενοι φοβερὸν, φλέγματι πάλιν καὶ αἵματι, χολῇ τε αὖ ξανθῇ καὶ μελαίνῃ, καὶ ὁλκῇ ἀέρος, καὶ τροφῇ αἰσθητῇ πρὸς τὸ ζῆν συνέχεσθαι μέλλειν τὰ σώματα κατὰ τὴν ἀνάστασιν, οὐδενὸς τὸ σύνολον ξένου παρὰ τὴν παροῦσαν ζωὴν διὰ τῆς ἀναστάσεως ἀναφανησομένου, πλὴν τὸ μὴ δύνασθαι πάλιν ἀποθανεῖν· οὐκ οἶδ᾿ ὅπως πρὸς πάντα τὰ ἐν τῇ ἁγίᾳ Γραφῇ περί τε ψυχῆς καὶ ἀναστάσεως νεκρῶν διά τε προφητῶν καὶ ἀποστόλων, καὶ αὐτοῦ τοῦ διὰ σαρκὸς ὁμιλήσαντος ἡμῖν Θεοῦ Λόγου, καὶ μάλιστα Κορινθίοις ἰδιοτρόπως διὰ τοῦ θεσπεσίου Παύλου περὶ ἀναστάσεως διηγορευμένα, τήν τε ἀκοὴν ἑκουσίως βύσαντες, καὶ πρὸς κατανόησιν τὸ τῆς ψυχῆς ὄμμα ἐπιμύσαντες, οὕτω τρανὰ ὑπάρχοντα καὶ ἐξάκουστα καὶ μηδενὸς τοῦ ἑρμηνεύοντος πρὸς τὸ γνωσθῆναι δεόμενα· καὶ τὴν φύσιν αὐτὴν τῶν ὄντων οὐχ ἧττον τῶν θειωδῶς ἐκπεφασμένων παιδεύουσαν ἔχοντες· ἣ καὶ τοὺς Βαρβάρους πρὸς γνῶσιν ἀληθείας ἐνάγειν ἐπίσταται, ἀρίδηλον ἑαυτὴν προβαλλομένη 436τῶν ζητουμένων ἀπόδειξιν, οὐκ αἰδούμενα. Τίς γὰρ, εἴπερ μὴ πάντῃ τῆς κατὰ τὸν λόγον ἐστέρηται χάριτος, ἀγνοεῖ, ὅτι πᾶσα φύσις οὐσιωδῶς οἰκείοις ἰδιώμασι διειλημμένη· καὶ ταύτῃ τόν τε τρόπον τῆς οἰκείας ὑπάρξεως, καὶ τὴν διαφορὰν καθ᾿ ἣν τῶν ἄλλων ἀμιγῶς διώρισται δεικνύουσα φύσεων· ἐπὰν τῶν συνεκτικῶν τῆς οὐσίας αὐτῆς ἀπογένηται ἰδιωμάτων, ἢ οὐδ᾿ ὅλως ἔσται, ἢ ὅπερ οὐκ ἦν, γέγονεν· εἴπερ ὅλως κατὰ τὴν ἀναίρεσιν τῶν ἰδιωμάτων εἶναι συμπεριφορικῶς αὐτὴν ἀνάσχοιτο εἰπεῖν. Πέφυκε γὰρ πᾶν ὑποκείμενον τῆς φυσικοῖς ἰδιώμασι φθειρομένοις συμφθείρεσθαι. Τί γὰρ, ἢ ποῦ βοῦς ἔσται, καὶ ἵππος, καὶ λέων, καὶ τὰ τοιαῦτα, τῶν συνεκτικῶν τῆς ἑκάστου φύσεως ἰδιωμάτων οὐκ ὄντων; Ἢ τίς εἶναι ταῦτα ἐκείνων χωρὶς εἰπεῖν θαρρήσοι, μανίας ὑπάρχων ἐλεύθερος, οὐ συνορῶ. Εἰ οὖν ἀληθὲς, τὸ μηδὲν δύνασθαι τῶν ὄντων εἶναι ἢ γνωσθῆναι ἄνευ τῶν φυσικῶς καὶ κυρίως αὐτὰ χαρακτηριζόντων, οἱ τῆς ψυχῆς τὸ κατ᾿ οὐσίαν αὐτῇ ἐμπεφυκὸς ἀφαιρούμενοι· τουτέστι, τὸ λογικόν τε καὶ νοερὸν, καθὸ ἀεί τέ ἐστι καὶ ἐνεργεῖ νοοῦσα καὶ λογιζομένη, προδήλως καὶ τὸ εἶναι αὐτῆς, κἂν μὴ λέγουσι, συναφαιροῦνται· καὶ ἔσται κατ᾿ αὐτοὺς, φθαρτὴ καὶ θνητὴ, τὸ εἶναι μετὰ τὸν τοῦ σώματος θάνατόν οὐκ ἔχουσα· οὗ τί ἀτοπώτερον; Εἰ δὲ ζῆν αὐτὴν ὑποτίθενται, κἂν λόγῳ, εἰ καὶ μὴ πράγματι, τὸν παρὰ πάντων ὑφορώμενοι Ἔλεγχον, πάντως καὶ κινεῖται. Πᾶσα γὰρ ζωὴ τῶν γενητῶν, ἐν κινήσει δείκνυται. Εἰ δὲ κινεῖται, καὶ ἐνεργεῖ Ven. Blac. ἐνεργεῖται. πάντως· πᾶσα γὰρ κίνησις, δι᾿ ἐνεργείας ἐκφαίνεται. Εἰ δὲ ἐνεργεῖ, φυσικῶς πάντως κινουμένη, καὶ οὐ θετικῶς, ἢ κατὰ συμβεβηκὸς, ἐνεργήσει. Οὐ γὰρ κυκλοφορικῶς, ἢ μεταβατικῶς· ἢ, συνελόντα εἰπεῖν, σωματικῶς· ἀλλὰ νοερῶς τε καὶ λογικῶς. Εἰ δὲ νοερῶς τε καὶ λογικῶς καὶ ζῇ καὶ κινεῖται καὶ ἐνεργεῖ, λογίζεται πάντως καὶ νοεῖ καὶ γινώσκει. Εἰ δὲ μὴ γινώσκει καὶ λογίζεται, οὐδὲ ἐνεργεῖ, οὐδὲ κινεῖται, οὐδὲ ζῇ. Οὐ γὰρ οἷόν τε εἶναι ζωὴν ἄνευ τῆς ἐμφύτου κινήσεως· οὐδὲ κίνησιν φυσικὴν, προσφυοῦς ἐνεργείας οὐκ οὔσης, ἐμφαίνεσθαι. Ἄλλως τε δὲ, ἡ ψυχὴ, ἢ δι᾿ ἑαυτὴν ἐστι λογικὴ τε καὶ νοερὰ, ἢ διὰ τὸ σῶμα. Καὶ εἰ μὲν δι᾿ ἑαυτὴν, ἤτοι τὴν ἑαυτῆς οὐσίαν ἐστὶ λογικὴ τε καὶ νοερὰ, καὶ αὐθυπόστατος, πάντως ἐστίν. Εἰ δὲ αὐθυπόστατος δι᾿ ἑαυτὴν φύσει καὶ καθ᾿ ἑαυτὴν, καὶ μετὰ σώματος ἐνεργήσει, νοοῦσα κατὰ φύσιν καὶ λογιζομένη, καὶ οὐδέποτε παυομένη τῶν φυσικῶς αὐτῇ προσουσῶν νοερῶν δυνάμεων. Τὰ γὰρ φύσει τῷ ὁπωσοῦν ὄντι προσόντα, ἕως ἐστὶ καὶ ὑφέστηκεν, ἀναφαίρετα τυγχάνει.

I know not how, toward all the things in the holy Scripture about the soul and the resurrection of the dead, spoken through prophets and apostles, and through the very Word of God who conversed with us through the flesh, and especially those set forth in a particular manner to the Corinthians through the divine Paul concerning the resurrection, I Cor. xv, 44. they have willingly stopped their hearing, and shut the eye of the soul to understanding — things so clear, and plain to hear, and needing no interpreter to be known; and, having nature itself, the nature of beings, teaching them no less than the things divinely declared — nature, which knows how to lead even the barbarians to knowledge of truth, 436putting itself forth as a manifest demonstration of the things sought — they are not abashed before her.

For who, unless he is wholly deprived of the grace of reason, does not know that every nature is essentially bounded by its own idioms, and by this shows both the mode of its own existence and the difference by which it is marked off without mixture from the other natures; and that when it is bereft of the idioms that hold together its essence, either it will not be at all, or it has become what it was not — if indeed one would consent at all, by way of accommodation, to say that it is, upon the removal of its idioms? For every subject is of a nature to be corrupted together with the natural idioms when they are corrupted. For what, or where, will an ox be, or a horse, or a lion, and such things, if the idioms holding together the nature of each are not? Or who, being free of madness, would dare to say that these things are without those, I do not see. If, then, it is true that none of the things that are can be or be known without the things that naturally and properly characterize them, then those who take away from the soul what is innate in it according to essence — that is, the rational and intellectual, in that it ever is, and operates understanding and reasoning — plainly take away together its being too, even if they do not say so; and according to them it will be corruptible and mortal, not having being after the death of the body: than which what is more absurd?

But if they suppose that it lives, in word at least if not in fact, wary of the refutation from all, then it is altogether also moved. For every life of things that have come to be is shown in movement. And if it is moved, it altogether also operates; for every movement is made manifest through operation. And if it operates, being moved altogether naturally, and not by imposition or by accident, it will operate — for not in a circle, or by change of place, or, to put it briefly, bodily, but intellectually and rationally. And if it both lives and is moved and operates intellectually and rationally, it altogether reasons and understands and knows. But if it does not know and reason, neither does it operate, nor is it moved, nor does it live. For it is not possible for there to be life without the innate movement; nor for natural movement to be shown forth when the connatural operation is not.

And besides: the soul is rational and intellectual either through itself, or through the body. And if through itself, that is, through its own essence, it is rational and intellectual, it is altogether also self-subsistent. And if it is self-subsistent through itself by nature, it will operate both by itself and with the body, understanding and reasoning according to nature, and never ceasing from the intellectual powers that belong to it naturally. For the things that belong by nature to what in any way is cannot be taken away so long as it is and subsists.

Ἡ ψυχὴ οὖν ἀεί τε οὖσα, ἀφ᾿ οὗ γέγονεν, καὶ ὑφισταμένη διὰ τὸν οὕτως 437αὐτὴν δημιουργήσαντα Θεὸν, ἀεὶ νοεῖ καὶ λογίζεται καὶ γινώσκει, καὶ καθ᾿ ἑαυτὴν, καὶ μετὰ σώματος, δι᾿ ἑαυτὴν καὶ τὴν ἑαυτῆς φύσιν. Οὐδεὶς οὖν εὑρεθήσεται λόγος, ὁ τὴν ψυχὴν τῶν προσόντων αὐτῇ φυσικῶς, καὶ οὐ διὰ τὸ σῶμα, μετὰ τὴν τούτου λύσιν ἀλλοτριῶσαι δυνάμενος. Εἰ δὲ διὰ τὸ σῶμα λογικὴ τε καὶ νοερά ἐστιν ἡ ψυχὴ, πρῶτον μὲν, τιμιώτερον τῆς δι᾿ αὐτὸ γενομένης ψυχῆς ἔσται τὸ σῶμα. Ἔπειτα δὲ, καὶ ἐξ αὐτοῦ ἕξει τό τε νοερὸν καὶ λογικὸν, ὡς δι᾿ αὐτὸ γενομένη. Εἰ γὰρ σώματος χωρὶς, οὐδαμῶς νοεῖν ἢ λογίζεσθαι δύναται ἡ ψυχὴ, ἐξ αὐτοῦ πάντως τὸ νοεῖν αὐτῇ καὶ λογίζεσθαι προσέσται. Εἰ δὲ τὸ νοεῖν καὶ λογίζεσθαι ἐκ τοῦ σώματος κέκτηται ἡ ψυχὴ, ὡς αὐτοῦ χωρὶς ταῦτα ἔχειν μὴ δυναμένη κατὰ τοὺς λέγοντας, οὐδὲ αὐθυπόστατος ἔσται πάντως· πῶς γὰρ ἡ ἄνευ σώματος καθ᾿ ἑαυτὴν τὸ χαρακτηρίζον οὐκ ἔχουσα; Εἰ δὲ αὐθυπόστατος οὐκ ἔστιν, οὐδὲ οὐσία ἐστὶ δηλονότι. Οὐσία δὲ αὐθυπόστατος μὴ οὖσα; συμβεβηκὸς ἔσται, φυσικῶς μόνον ὑφισταμένῳ τῷ σώματι ἐνυπάρχουσα· μετὰ δὲ τὴν αὐτοῦ λύσιν, οὐδὲν οὐδαμῶς εἶναι δυναμένη· καὶ οὐδὲν ἔσται πλέον τοῖς τοσοῦτον ἀνοηταίνουσι, τὴν ἀθανασίαν τῆς ψυχῆς ἀφῃρημένοις, τῆς Ἐπικούρου καὶ Ἀριστοτέλους ματαιοπονίας, οἷς, ὡς εἰκὸς, οἱ γεννάδαι, καὶ σεμνύνονται συντασσόμενοι. Καὶ περὶ μὲν τούτου, ταῦτα. Τοῦ δὲ περὶ ἀναστάσεως παρ᾿ αὐτῶν λεγομένου, τίς ἀρετῆς καὶ εὐσεβείας ἐπιμελούμενος ἀκούων, οὐχὶ τὸ ἀπαθὲς ἑαυτῷ διὰ πάθους, τῆς κατ᾿ αὐτῶν ἀγανακτήσεως λέγω, περιποιήσεται; Εἰ γὰρ, ὥς φασι, κατὰ τὴν εἰκόνα τῆς παρούσης ζωῆς, ἀπαραλλάκτως τοῖς μαχομένοις χυμοῖς πεποιωμένον ἀναστήσεται τὸ σῶμα, καὶ τραφήσεται ἐν τῇ φοβερᾷ τοῦ Θεοῦ παρουσίᾳ μετὰ τὴν τοῦ κόσμου τούτου συντέλειαν, οὐκ οἶμαι ἄλλο τι αὐτοὺς διὰ τούτων, ἵνα συνελὼν εἴπω, τὰ πολλὰ περικόψας ὅσα ῥηθῆναι ἐνδέχεται, ἢ ἀΐδιον τὸν θάνατον πρεσβεύειν, καὶ τὴν φθορὰν ἀτελεύτητον.

The soul, then, ever being since it came to be, and subsisting through God who 437fashioned it so, ever understands and reasons and knows, both by itself and with the body, through itself and its own nature. No account, therefore, will be found that can estrange the soul, after the body's dissolution, from the things that belong to it naturally, and not on account of the body. But if the soul is rational and intellectual on account of the body, first, the body will be more honourable than the soul that came to be on account of it; then, too, it will have the intellectual and rational from it, as having come to be on account of it. For if without the body the soul can in no way understand or reason, understanding and reasoning will belong to it altogether from the body. And if the soul has acquired understanding and reasoning from the body, as being unable, according to those who say so, to have these without it, it will altogether not be self-subsistent either; for how could it be, which without the body does not have by itself what characterizes it? And if it is not self-subsistent, it is clearly not an essence either. And not being a self-subsistent essence, it will be an accident, inhering only in the body that subsists naturally, and after its dissolution able in no way to be anything at all. And there will be nothing more, for those who are so senseless as to have taken away the immortality of the soul, than the vain labour of Epicurus and Aristotle, with whom, as is likely, these noble fellows even pride themselves on being ranked. And on this, so much.

But as to what is said by them about the resurrection, who that cares for virtue and piety, hearing it, will not gain dispassion for himself through a passion — I mean indignation against them? For if, as they say, the body will rise according to the image of the present life, qualified invariantly by the warring humours, and will be fed at the fearful coming of God after the consummation of this world, I do not think that through these things — to speak briefly, cutting short the many things that might be said — they champion anything other than eternal death and corruption without end.

Εἰ γὰρ φθορὰ τῶν οὕτω συνισταμένων ὑπάρχει ὁ θάνατος σωμάτων, ἀεὶ δὲ τὸ δι᾿ 440ἐπιῤῥοῆς τροφῶν συνιστάμενον φθείρεται σῶμα, τῇ ῥοῇ διαπνεόμενον, διὰ τὴν τῶν ἐν αὐτῷ χυμῶν, ἐξ ὧν καὶ συνέστηκε, φυσικὴν ἀντιπάθειαν· ἀεὶ ἄρα, δι᾿ ὧν μετὰ τὴν ἀνάστασιν τὸ σῶμα συνίστασθαι ὑποτίθενται, συντηρούμενον εἰς τὸ διηνεκὲς τὸν θάνατον καταγγέλλουσι. Δέον πιστεύειν, αὐτὸ μὲν ἀνίστασθαι κατ᾿ οὐσίαν καὶ εἶδος τὸ σῶμα, ἄφθαρτον δὲ καὶ ἀθάνατον, καὶ ἀποστολικῶς εἰπεῖν, ἀντὶ ψυχικοῦ πνευματικόν· τῷ μηδὲν φέρειν τῆς ἀλλοιωτῆς αὐτοῦ κατὰ τὴν φθορὰν συστατικῆς ἰδιότητος, ὡς οἶδεν ὁ πρὸς τὸ ἀπαθὲς, καὶ τίμιον αὐτὸ μετακεράσων Θεός· καὶ μηδὲν πλέον περιεργάζεσθαι. Τούτοις χαίροντας καὶ ἡδομένους ὁρῶν τοὺς πολλούς, Ἐκωφώθην, καὶ ἐταπεινώθην, καὶ ἐσίγησα ἐξ ἀγαθῶν· λυσιτελεστέραν τοῦ λόγου τὴν σιωπὴν παντὶ συνετῷ εἶναι κρίνας· καὶ κατ᾿ ἐμαυτὸν πενθῶν, καὶ σκυθρωπάζων πορεύομαι· λογιζόμενος πόσον οἱ τοιοῦτοι λόγοι ἀμελούμενοι, πανταχοῦ σπινθήρων δίκην διᾴττοντες, ψυχῶν ἐργάσονται θάνατον· καὶ οὐδείς ἐστιν ὁ ἐπαμῦναι τῷ λόγῳ δυνάμενος, ἢ βουλόμενος κινδυνεύοντι, διὰ τὴν ἐπικρατοῦσαν τοῦ καιροῦ πονηρίαν. Διὸ καὶ μᾶλλον στένω τὸν ἀφ᾿ ὑμῶν χωρισμὸν, τὴν ἐγγύθεν φρουροῦσάν με καὶ ἐξαιρουμένην τῶν βλαβερῶν πανσθενῆ τῆς ὑμῶν πατρικῆς ἁγιωσύνης ἐπισκοπὴν οὐκ ἔχων, συχνότερον τῷ λόγῳ τὰ πολλά μου ἐρείδουσαν ὀλισθήματα.

Η΄. — Τοῦ αὐτοῦ πρὸς τὸν αὐτόν. Ὁ κατὰ σάρκα πόθος χρόνῳ μαραίνεσθαι πέφυκε, συναπιῶν χωριζομένοις τοπικῶς, τοῖς κατ᾿ αὐτὴν πρὸς ἀλλήλους ἔχουσι τὴν συνάφειαν. Περὶ γὰρ τὴν αἴσθησιν ἔχει τὴν σύστασιν, τὴν μηδαμῶς τῶν μὴ παρόντων ἀντιλαμβάνεσθαι δυναμένην. Ὁ δὲ κατὰ πνεῦμα πόθος, ἀεὶ συμπαρόντας ἀλλήλοις ἔχει νοερῶς, τοὺς κατ᾿ αὐτὸν συνημμένους, κἂν σωματικῶς ἀλλήλων χωρισθῶσι· τὴν κατὰ χρόνον ἢ τόπον περιγραφὴν οὐ δεχόμενος. Περὶ γὰρ τὸν νοῦν ἔχει τὴν ὕπαρξιν, τὸν μηδαμῶς τοῖς σώμασιν ἀλλήλων διαιρουμένοις τοπικῶς, συνδιαιρούμενον ἢ συμπερικλειόμενον.

For if death is the corruption of bodies so constituted, and the body that is constituted 440through the influx of foods is ever corrupted, dissipated by the flux on account of the natural antipathy of the humours in it, of which it is also constituted, then by the very things through which they suppose the body to be constituted after the resurrection they proclaim death preserved forever. Whereas one ought to believe that the body itself rises according to essence and form, but incorruptible and immortal, and, to speak apostolically, spiritual instead of psychic, I Cor. xv, 44. in that it carries nothing of the constitutive property, alterable according to corruption, as God knows who will temper it anew toward the impassible and honourable; and to busy oneself about nothing more.

Seeing the many rejoicing and delighting in these things, I was dumb, and was humbled, and kept silence from good things, Psal. xxxviii, 3. judging silence more profitable than speech for every prudent man; and I go mourning and with a sad countenance by myself, reckoning how great a death of souls such words, if neglected, will work, darting everywhere like sparks; and there is no one able or willing to come to the help of the word in its danger, on account of the wickedness of the time that prevails. Wherefore I groan the more at my separation from you, not having close at hand the all-powerful oversight of your paternal holiness, which guarded me and delivered me from harmful things, and more frequently propped up by word my many slips.

LETTER 8

Of the same, to the same.

The longing according to the flesh is of a nature to wither with time, departing along with those who are separated in place, who have their conjunction with one another according to it. For it has its constitution in sense, which can in no way lay hold of the things not present. But the longing according to the spirit ever holds intellectually present to one another those joined by it, even if they are separated bodily from one another, not admitting circumscription by time or place. For it has its existence in the mind, which is in no way divided along with, or shut in along with, the bodies divided from one another in place.

Τοῦτον τὸν πόθον ἀπ᾿ ἀρχῆς πρὸς τοὺς ἁγιωτάτους 441ὑμᾶς ἔχειν ἀξιωθεὶς, ἀεὶ παρόντας ὁρᾶν δοκῶ, καὶ διαλεγομένων αἰσθάνεσθαι· καὶ οὐκ ἔστι καιρὸς ἢ τόπος, ὁ ἀφιστᾶν με δυνάμενος τῆς ὑμετέρας μνήμης· διὰ παντὸς ὑμᾶς παρόντας πνευματικῶς ὑποδεικνυούσης, καὶ πάντας τοὺς ἐν ἐμοὶ δυσώδεις λογισμοὺς ἀπελαύνοντας, τῆς ἐν ὑμῖν θείας χάριτος τὴν πολλὴν εὐωδίαν οὐ φέροντας· καὶ πείθομαί γε, μὴ ψιλῶς τὴν μνήμην φαντάζεσθαι τοὺς ἁγιωτάτους ὑμᾶς, ἀλλὰ παρόντων ἀληθῶς ἐπαισθάνεσθαι· τὸ γινόμενον πληροφορίαν ἀκριβῆ τῆς ὑμῶν παρουσίας ποιούμενος. Ἡ γὰρ ἐν ὑμῖν κατὰ χάριν Θεοῦ δραστήριος δύναμις, ἅμα τῇ μνήμῃ τοὺς διοχλοῦντας ἀπελαύνουσα δαίμονας, σαφεστάτην τῆς ὑμῶν παρουσίας παρέχεται δήλωσιν. Καὶ θαυμαστὸν οὐδὲν, εἰ τοὺς ἀπόντας σωματικῶς, παρεῖναι ποιεῖ νοερῶς, ὁ τῶν σημείων καὶ τῶν τεράτων Θεός· ὡς οἶδεν αὐτὸς, καὶ οἱ ἐξ αὐτοῦ τὰ τοιαῦτα θεοπρεπῶς ἐνεργούμενοι· καὶ πλέον ἢ ὅσον τοπικῶς ἀλλήλοις τὰ σώματα πάρεστι. Καὶ εἰ μόνον μνημονευόμενοι, τίμιοι Πατέρες, τοσοῦτόν μου τοῖς ἀτίμοις ἐπιτίμιον παρέχετε λογισμοῖς, ἀοράτως φοιτῶντες τῷ πνεύματι, πόσῳ μᾶλλον κατ᾿ ὀφθαλμοὺς παρόντες, καὶ τὴν ἐμὴν ἀκοὴν διὰ ζώσης φωνῆς τοῖς θείοις καθαγιάζοντες λόγοις, καὶ τὴν ἀρετὴν τοῖς ἑαυτῶν τρόποις λαμπρῶς ἐκδιδάσκοντες; Διὰ τοῦτο πενθῶν καὶ σκυθρωπάζων πορεύομαι, ποθῶν τε καὶ ζητῶν σε τὸν καλὸν ποιμένα καὶ διδάσκαλον, τὸν εἰς τόπον με χλόης τὸ πλανώμενον πρόβατον κατασκηνοῦν ἐπιστάμενον· τὴν ἀπωθουμένην λέγω τὸν καύσωνα τῶν παθῶν διὰ πράξεως ἕξιν τῆς ἀρετῆς· καὶ ἐπὶ ὕδατος ἀναπαύσεως ἐκτρέφειν δυνάμενον· τὴν ἄρδουσάν φημι τὸν νοῦν διὰ θεωρίας χάριν τῆς γνώσεως, τὸν ἐπιστρέφοντά μου τὴν ψυχὴν ἀπὸ κακίας εἰς ἀρετὴν θείοις καὶ λόγοις καὶ παραδείγμασι, καὶ ἐπὶ τρίβους ὁδηγοῦντα δικαιοσύνης· τοὺς κατ᾿ εὐσέβειαν σωτηριώδεις ὑποδεικνύοντα τρόπους· καὶ ῥάβδῳ καὶ βακτηρίᾳ σοφῶς παρακαλοῦντα, τουτέστι, τῇ μνήμῃ τῶν αἰωνίων κολάσεων, καθάπερ ῥάβδῳ τινὶ τὸν εἰσαγόμενον κακίας ἀπάγοντα, καὶ τῇ προτροπῇ τῶν μελλόντων ἀγαθῶν, βακτηρίας δίκην εἰς ἀρετὴν τὸν προκόπτοντα δι᾿ ἐλπίδος στηρίζοντα· ὅπερ καὶ ὁ Κύριος καὶ Σωτὴρ, ὡς οἶμαι, συμβολικῶς ὑπηνίττετο, τοῖς τραύμασι τοῦ τοῖς λησταῖς περιπεσόντος ἐπιχέων οἶνον καὶ ἔλαιον. Φόβῳ γὰρ τῆς γεέννης τοῦ αἰωνίου πυρὸς, ὁ τῆς βασιλείας τῶν οὐρανῶν πόθος ἐπικιρνώμενος, ἀλώβητον τὴν ψυχὴν συντηρεῖ, πρὸς τὴν ἀγάπην ἐπισφίγγων τοῦ Κτίσαντος· πάσης ἐμπαθοῦς διανοίας διὰ τῆς στύψεως ἀποσμηχθεῖσαν.

Having been deemed worthy from the beginning to have this longing toward you most holy ones, 441I seem ever to see you present, and to perceive you conversing; and there is no time or place that can remove me from the memory of you, which shows you constantly present spiritually, and you driving away all the foul-smelling thoughts in me, which do not bear the great fragrance of the divine grace in you. And I am persuaded indeed that memory does not merely imagine you most holy ones, but perceives you truly present; making what comes to pass an exact assurance of your presence. For the effective power in you according to the grace of God, driving away together with the memory the demons that trouble me, provides the clearest showing of your presence. And it is nothing wondrous if the God of signs and wonders makes those absent bodily to be present intellectually — as he himself knows, and those who are operated by him in such things in a manner befitting God — and more than bodies are present to one another in place. And if, merely remembered, honoured Fathers, you furnish so great a penalty to my dishonourable thoughts, visiting invisibly in the spirit, how much more when present before my eyes, and sanctifying my hearing with divine words by a living voice, and teaching virtue brilliantly by your own manners?

Therefore I go mourning and with a sad countenance, longing for and seeking thee, the good shepherd and teacher, who knowest how to make me, the wandering sheep, dwell in a place of green pasture Psal. xxii, 2. — I mean the habit of virtue through practice, which drives back the burning heat of the passions — and who art able to nourish me beside the water of rest — I say the grace of knowledge that waters the mind through contemplation; who turnest my soul from vice to virtue by divine words and examples, and leadest me in the paths of righteousness, showing the saving modes according to piety; and who wisely comfortest me with rod and staff — that is, by the memory of the everlasting punishments, leading the beginner away from vice as with a kind of rod, and by the encouragement of the good things to come, supporting through hope, like a staff, the one who is advancing toward virtue; which, I think, the Lord and Saviour too hinted symbolically, pouring wine and oil on the wounds of him who fell among robbers. Luc. x, 34. For the longing for the kingdom of heaven, mixed with the fear of the Gehenna of everlasting fire, keeps the soul unhurt, binding it fast to the love of the Creator, once it has been scoured of every passionate thought through the astringency.

Ἀλλὰ πλήρωσον λοιπὸν, τίμιε Πάτερ, καὶ αὐτὸς Πνεύματος Θείου τῆς διδασκαλίας τὸν δόνακα, 444καὶ κάλει ποιμενικῶς τε καὶ φιλανθρώπως τὸν ἐπὶ τὰ ὄρη τῆς ἀγνοίας, καὶ τοὺς βουνοὺς τῆς κακίας, ἀποιμάντως πλανώμενον· καὶ ὑπὸ τὴν σὴν ποίησον δεξιὰν, καὶ κατάδησον συμπαθῶς τὸν πολλοῖς διασπαραχθέντα δήγμασι πονηροῖς τῶν λύκων τῆς Ἀραβίας· τουτέστι τῶν Δυσμῶν· Δυσμὰς γὰρ, ὡς φασι, δηλοῖ κατὰ τὴν Ἑλλάδα φωνὴν ἑρμηνευόμενον τῆς Ἀραβίας τὸ ὄνομα. Νοῶ δὲ, καὶ τυχὸν οὐχ ἐσφαλμένως, κατὰ τοὺς τῆς ἀναγωγῆς λόγους, διὰ τῆς Ἀραβίας, τὴν σάρκα ταύτην, τὴν τῶν θείων ἐν τοῖς κατ᾿ ἐμὲ τυραννικὴν νόμων τοῦ πνεύματος· τὴν πολλοὺς, ὡς ἀληθῶς, ἀναιδεῖς τε καὶ ἀνημέρους λύκους ἐκτρέφουσαν· τὴν ὄντως δύσιν καὶ γενομένην διὰ τὴν ἁμαρτίαν, ἀληθῶς λεγομένην· ἧς ἐπιβέβηκεν, ὡς ἀγαθὸς καὶ φιλάνθρωπος, ὁ ποιμὴν ὁ καλὸς, ὁ ποιμαίνων τὸν Ἰσραὴλ Θεὸς, καὶ Θεοῦ Λόγος· ὁ καθήμενος ἐπὶ τῶν Χερουβὶμ, προσεσχηκώς δηλονότι, καὶ τὸν κίνδυνον ἡμῶν θεασάμενος· καὶ διὰ τοῦτο ἐμφανίσας ἑαυτὸν, καὶ τὴν οἰκείαν ἐξεγείρας δυναστείαν, καὶ ἐλθὼν ἐν σαρκὶ σῶσαι τοὺς ἀπολομένους, καὶ τοὺς ἐντρεφομένους αὐτῇ πονηροὺς ἀποκτεῖναι θῆρας· καὶ ποιῆσαι αὐτὴν χώραν εἰρήνης, καὶ λογικῶν προβάτων νομήν, διὰ πράξεως πρὸς Θεὸν ἀναγομένων· ἧς ἐγὼ τέως οὔπω τὸν νόμον παρῆλθον ἀβλαβῶς διὰ θεωρίας πνευματικῆς, καθάπερ ὁ Ἰσραὴλ τὸ ὄρος Σιείρ· οὐδὲ τὰς αὐτῆς ἀπαθῶς διῆλθον ἐπιθυμίας, διὰ πράξεως γνωστικῆς, ὥσπερ τοὺς ἀδελφοὺς αὐτῶν τοὺς υἱοὺς Ἡσαῦ Fr. Οἱ Ἄραβες ἐκ τοῦ Ἠσαῦ τὸ γένος κατάγονσι : Arabes ex Esau genus ducunt., τοὺς κατοικοῦντας ἐν Ἀραβᾷ διὰ Μωσέως, οἱ ἐξ Ἰακὼβ τὸ γένος κατάγοντες· ἀλλ᾿ ἔτι κατοικῶ τὴν Ἀραβᾷ κατὰ τοὺς υἱοὺς Ἡσαῦ, τὰς δυσμάς· λέγω δὲ τὴν σάρκα· μετὰ τὴν παροῦσαν ζωὴν, διὰ τὰς σαρκικὰς ἡδονὰς, ἑτέραν μὴ ἐκδεχόμενος. Ἣν οὐδὲ παροικῆσαι πᾶς ἀληθινὸς Ἰσραὴλ καὶ ὁρῶν Θεὸν καταδέχεται, τῆς σαρκὸς ἐκδημῆσαι διὰ τῶν ἀρετῶν ἐπειγόμενος, καὶ ἐνδημῆσαι πρὸς τὸν Κύριον διὰ τῆς γνώσεως ἐφιέμενος· τὸν ὄντως γῆν ἐπαγγελίας ἀῤῥήτων ἀγαθῶν μυστικῶς καὶ ὄντα καὶ ὠνομασμένον, τὴν ῥέουσαν γάλα καὶ μέλι. Θρεπτικὸς γὰρ τῶν κατ᾿ αὐτὸν νηπιαζόντων, καὶ εὐφραντικὸς ὑπάρχει τῶν κατ᾿ αὐτὸν ἀνδριζομένων ὁ Κύριος, ὡς ἀρεταῖς ἐκτρέφων διὰ πράξεως, καθάπερ γάλακτι, τοὺς φοβουμένους αὐτὴν, καὶ γνώσεσι καταγλυκαίνων μυστικαῖς, καθάπερ μέλιτι διὰ θεωρίας πνευματικῆς, τοὺς ἀγαπῶντας αὐτόν.

But fill now, honoured Father, thou too, the reed of teaching with the divine Spirit, 444and call, like a shepherd and with love for man, him who wanders unshepherded on the mountains of ignorance and the hills of vice; and put him under thy right hand, and bind up with sympathy him who has been torn by many wicked bites of the wolves of Arabia Habac. i, 8. — that is, of the West; for the name of Arabia, as they say, when interpreted into the Greek tongue, signifies "West". And I understand — and perhaps not mistakenly — according to the principles of anagogy, by Arabia this flesh, tyrannous in those like me over the divine laws of the spirit; which nourishes, in very truth, many shameless and savage wolves; which is truly a West, and has become one through sin, and is truly so called; on which there rides, Psal. lxvii, 5. as good and loving man, the good Shepherd, he who shepherds Israel, God and the Word of God; he who sits upon the Cherubim, having clearly given heed and beheld our danger; and therefore having shown himself, and roused up his own might, and come in the flesh to save the lost, and to slay the wicked beasts nurtured in it, and to make it a land of peace, and a pasture of rational sheep led up to God through practice. Of which I have not yet passed by the territory unharmed through spiritual contemplation, as Israel passed Mount Seir; nor have I passed through its desires dispassionately, through a practice of knowledge, as those who drew their race from Jacob passed, through Moses, their brothers the sons of Esau who dwelt in Araba; but I still dwell in Araba, like the sons of Esau — the West, I mean the flesh — expecting, on account of the fleshly pleasures, no other life after the present one. Which flesh every true Israel, one who sees God, does not consent even to sojourn in, pressing on to be away from the flesh through the virtues, and aspiring to be at home with the Lord II Cor. v, 6. through knowledge — him who mystically both is and is named the true land of promise of ineffable good things, that flows with milk and honey. For the Lord is nourisher of those who are infants according to him, and the delight of those who are growing to manhood according to him; nourishing with virtues through practice, as with milk, those who fear him, and sweetening with mystical knowledges, as with honey through spiritual contemplation, those who love him.

Τοιγαροῦν, μὴ διαλίπῃς ποιμαίνων με συμπαθῶς ἀληθείας λόγοις τὸν θηριάλωτον, τίμιε Πάτερ, καὶ 445τῶν ἐν σαρκὶ παθῶν μετὰ τῶν ἀνακινούντων αὐτὰ πνευμάτων τῆς πονηρίας, μὴ παύσῃ λυτρούμενος, τῶν κακῶς κατεσθιόντων καὶ δαπανώντων μου τὸν ἐντὸς ἄνθρωπον· καὶ κάλεσον πρὸς ἑαυτὸν, καὶ ὑπὸ τὰς σὰς κατασκήνωσον πτέρυγας, εἴπερ ἀληθῶς ἄπεστι πᾶς φόβος Βαρβάρων αἰσθητῶν, δι᾿ οὓς τοσαῦτα διαστήματα θαλάσσης, ὡς φιλόζωος, ἦλθον· περὶ οὗ μάλιστα παρακαλῶ τοὺς ἁγιωτάτους ὑμᾶς, μετὰ τὴν εὐκταίαν σημείωσιν τῆς κατὰ Θεὸν παμφαοῦς ὑμῶν ῥώσεως, ἀκριβέστερον δηλῶσαι· καὶ πᾶσαν τὴν κρατοῦσαν Fr. ἐπικρατοῦσαν. ἐπὶ τοῦ παρόντος αὐτόθι κατάστασιν, ὡς ἔστι, παραδεῖξαί μοι διὰ γραμμάτων. Ἀσθενὴς γὰρ ὢν τὸν λογισμὸν καὶ πάνυ σαθρὸς, καὶ μόλις κἂν ἐν εἰρήνῃ νήφειν μικρομερῶς δυνάμενος, καὶ περὶ πολλὰ σκεδαννύμενον ἐκ δειλίας συνάγειν τὸν νοῦν, θέλω σὺν ἀσφαλείᾳ ποιήσασθαι τὸν πλοῦν· ὡς ἀτελὴς τὴν διάνοιαν, καὶ τοῖς λόγοις τῆς σοφῶς τὸ πᾶν διεξαγούσης προνοίας ἐμβατεύειν οὐχ οἷός τε ὤν· μή πως ἐν τοῖς συμβαίνουσιν ἐξ ἀγνοίας ὀκλάσας τὸν λογισμὸν, καὶ τοῖς τῆς ὑπομονῆς ἐξ ἀσθενείας ἐνδοὺς ἀγῶσι, τοὺς ἐξ αὐτῶν ζημιωθῶ στεφάνους, ὡς λειποτάκτης ἐν τῇ πείρᾳ τῶν δεόντων For. δεινῶν. γενόμενος. Διὰ τοῦ γράμματος ὡς παρὼν ἀσπάζομαι τοὺς ἁγιωτάτους ὑμᾶς, καὶ πάντας τοὺς σὺν ὑμῖν· αἰτῶν διὰ τῶν ἁγίων καὶ εὐπροσδέκτων ὑμῶν εὐχῶν, Χριστῷ τῷ Θεῷ καὶ Σωτῆρι τῶν ὅλων παρατεθῆναι.

Θ΄. — Τοῦ αὐτοῦ πρὸς Θαλλάσιον πρεσβύτερον καὶ ἡγούμενον. Τρία, καθώς φασιν, ὑπάρχουσι, τὰ τὸν ἄνθρωπον ἄγοντα· μᾶλλον δὲ πρὸς ἃ βουλήσει τε καὶ γνώμῃ κατὰ προαίρεσιν κινεῖται ὁ ἄνθρωπος· Θεὸς, καὶ φύσις, καὶ κόσμος. Καὶ τούτων ἕκαστον ἕλκον, τῶν ἄλλων δύο ἐξίστησι, πρὸς ἑαυτὸ ἀλλοιοῦν τὸν ἀγόμενον· κἀκεῖνο ποιοῦν αὐτὸν θέσει, ὅπερ αὐτὸ ὑπάρχον φύσει γνωρίζεται, πλὴν μέντοι τῆς φύσεως. Ἐκείνη γὰρ, τοῦθ᾿ ὅπερ ἐστὶ διαφυλάττει τὸν ἄνθρωπον, θεὸν ποιεῖν θέσει πέφυκε, θέωσιν ὡς ἀγαθὸς ὑπὲρ φύσιν τῷ ἀγομένῳ δωρούμενος· καὶ τῶν δύο καθαρῶς ἀποτέμνει, κόσμου λέγω καὶ φύσεως. Εἰ δὲ φύσις ἐστὶν ἡ ἄγουσα τὸν ἄνθρωπον, καθ᾿ ἑαυτὸν ὄντα, φύσει τὸν ἄνθρωπον διαδείκνυσι· μέσον Θεοῦ καὶ κόσμου τυγχάνουσα· ὡς οὐδετέρου τούτων κατὰ γνώμην μετέχοντα. Εἰ δὲ κόσμος ἐστὶν ὁ φέρων, κτῆνος ἐργάζεται, ἤτοι σάρκα μόνην τὸν ἀγόμενον ἄνθρωπον· ἐμπάθειαν αὐτῷ δι᾿ ἀπάτης δημιουργῶν· καθ᾿ ἣν Θεοῦ τε καὶ φύσεως μακρὰν αὐτὸν ἀποθέμενος, τὰ παρὰ φύσιν πάντα ποιεῖν αὐτὸν ἐκδιδάσκει. Αἱ οὖν ἀκρότητες, Θεὸν δὲ φημι καὶ κόσμον, ἀλλήλων καὶ τῆς μεσότητος, λέγω δὲ τὴν φύσιν, ἀπάγειν τὸν ἄνθρωπον εἰώθασιν.

Therefore do not cease, honoured Father, to shepherd me with sympathy by the words of truth — me, the prey of beasts — and do not stop ransoming me 445from the passions in the flesh, together with the spirits of wickedness that stir them up, which wickedly devour and consume my inner man; and call me to thyself, and make me dwell under thy wings, if indeed all fear of the sensible barbarians, on account of whom I came across such stretches of sea as one who loves life, is truly gone. About which I especially beseech you most holy ones, after the longed-for notice of your all-radiant health according to God, to inform me more exactly; and to show me by letter the whole state of affairs prevailing there at present, as it is. For being weak in thought and very rotten, and scarcely able even in peace to be sober in some small part, and to gather together my mind that is scattered about many things through cowardice, I wish to make the voyage with safety; as being imperfect in understanding, and not able to enter into the principles of the providence that wisely governs the whole; lest, bending at the knee in thought through ignorance in what befalls, and through weakness yielding in the contests of endurance, I be deprived of the crowns that come from them, having become a deserter in the trial of what is required. By the letter, as if present, I greet you most holy ones, and all those with you; asking, through your holy and acceptable prayers, to be commended to Christ, the God and Saviour of all.

LETTER 9

Of the same, to Thalassius, presbyter and abbot.

There are three things, as they say, that lead man; or rather, toward which man is moved by wish and inclination according to choice: God, and nature, and the world. And each of these, drawing him, removes him from the other two, altering the one led toward itself, and making him by adoption that which it is itself known to be by nature — except, however, nature. For that one — it preserves man as that which he is — is of a nature to make him god by adoption, bestowing deification, as good, above nature on the one led; and it cuts him off cleanly from the two, I mean the world and nature. But if it is nature that leads man, it shows man, being what he is in himself, man by nature, since she is the middle between God and the world, as one sharing in neither of these by inclination. But if it is the world that carries him, it works the man led into a beast, that is, flesh alone, fashioning in him through deceit a passionate state, by which, putting him far from God and nature, it teaches him to do all things contrary to nature.

The extremes, then — I mean God and the world — are wont to lead man away from one another and from the middle, I mean nature.

Ἡ δὲ μεσότης, τούτων 448οὖσα μεθόριος, εἰ αὐτὴν μόνην σκοποῦντα λάβοι τὸν ἄνθρωπον, ἐπίσης τῶν ἄκρων διίστησιν· οὔτε πρὸς τὸν Θεὸν αὐτὸν ἀναδραμεῖν συγχωροῦσα, καὶ πρὸς τὸν κόσμον ἀφιέναι καταπεσεῖν αἰδουμένη. Ἅμα τοίνυν πρός τινα τούτων κινηθῇ κατὰ γνώμην ἐνδιαθέτως ὁ ἄνθρωπος, ἅμα πρὸς αὐτὸν ἐκεῖνον καὶ τὴν ἐνέργειαν ἥμειψε, καὶ τὴν προσηγορίαν μετέβαλε, σαρκικὸς, ἢ ψυχικὸς, ἢ πνευματικὸς προσαγορευόμενος. Ἔργον δὲ καὶ γνώρισμα τοῦ μὲν σαρκικοῦ, τὸ κακῶς μόνον εἰδέναι ποιεῖν. Τοῦ δὲ ψυχικοῦ, τὸ μήτε ποιεῖν βούλεσθαί ποτε, μήτε πάσχειν κακῶς. Τοῦ δὲ πνευματικοῦ, τὸ ποιεῖν μόνον καλῶς βούλεσθαι, καὶ ὑπὲρ ἀρετῆς εἰ συμβαίνει πάσχειν καλῶς, προθύμως καταδέχεσθαι. Εἰ τοίνυν Πνεύματι Θεοῦ ἄγεσθαι ποθεῖς, εὐλογημένε, ὥσπερ οὖν καὶ ποθεῖς, κόσμον καὶ φύσιν σαυτοῦ περίελε· μᾶλλον δὲ τούτων σαυτὸν περίτεμε, καὶ ἀδικεῖσθαι μὴ παραιτήσῃ· ἐμπαιγμούς τε καὶ ὕβρεις φέρειν μὴ ἀπαναίνου· καὶ ἵνα συνελὼν εἴπω, πάσχων κακῶς, τοῦ ποιεῖν καλῶς τοῖς δρῶσι κακῶς, καὶ πάντα προσαφιέναι τὰ δρώμενα, Θεοῦ χάριν καὶ ἀρετῆς, μὴ παύσῃ ποτὲ, κατὰ τὸν εἰπόντα· Ἐάν τις θέλῃ σοι κριθῆναι, καὶ τὸν χιτῶνά σου λαβεῖν, ἄφες αὐτῷ καὶ τὸ ἱμάτιον. Καὶ πάλιν κατὰ τὸν μακάριον Ἀπόστολον, λέγοντα· Λοιδορούμενοι, εὐλογοῦμεν· διωκόμενοι, ἀνεχόμεθα· βλασφημούμενοι, παρακαλοῦμεν. Λοιπὸν εἰ πεισθῆναί μοι βούλοιο, δοῦλε τοῦ Θεοῦ εὐλογημένε, μεγάλην κατάθου χάριν τοῖς θλίψασι· καὶ προσζημιωθῆναι αὐτοῖς εἰ δέον ἐστὶ πάντα, μὴ ἀνανεύσῃς· καὶ μᾶλλον εὐλόγησον λοιδορούμενος· καὶ ἀνάσχου διωκόμενος, καὶ παρακάλεσον βλασφημούμενος, ἵνα μὴ ᾖς σαρκικὸς, ὡς ἀδικεῖν μόνον καὶ εἰδὼς καὶ βουλόμενος· ἢ ψυχικὸς, ὡς ἀδικεῖσθαι μὴ ἀνεχόμενος· ἀλλὰ μᾶλλον πνευματικὸς, τὸ εὖ ποιεῖν μόνον ἑκουσίως καὶ εἰδὼς καὶ ἀσκούμενος· καὶ πάσχειν κακῶς παρὰ τῶν βουλομένων προθύμως ὑπὲρ ἀρετῆς ἐπιστάμενος· ἀφορῶν πρὸς τὸν τῆς σωτηρίας ἡμῶν ἀρχηγὸν Ἰησοῦν· ὃς ἀντὶ πάντων τῶν ἀγαθῶν, ὅσα οὐδέπω τις γνώσει περιλαβεῖν δεδύνηται, πάντα τὰ δεινὰ, ὅσα οὐδεὶς ὑπὸ τῶν ἁμαρτωλῶν, καὶ ὑπὲρ τῶν ἁμαρτωλῶν μακροθύμως ὑπέμεινε. Σκοπὸς γὰρ τῷ δοτῆρι τῶν ἐντολῶν, κόσμου καὶ φύσεως ἐλευθερῶσαι τὸν ἄνθρωπον. Καὶ διὰ τοῦτο κατάκριτος, ὁ μὴ πειθόμενος· καὶ μάτην οἱ κατὰ κόσμον πατέρες τὰ ἔκγονα προφασίζονται, καὶ τὰς συνοδίας τῶν τὸν μονήρη βίον ἀσκουμένων οἱ προϊστάμενοι, πρὸς τὸ δεῖν εὐλόγως αὐτοῖς ἀργὰς εἶναι τὰς ἐντολάς. Ὅπερ ὡς ἀληθὲς εἰ δεξοίμεθα, οὐδενὶ τὸ παράπαν ὁ Κύριος γεγραφὼς νόμον σωτηρίας δειχθήσεται. Ψυχικὸν δὲ, καθώς οἶμαι, τὸν φυσικὸν ὁ τῆς Γραφῆς λόγος ἐκάλεσεν ἄνθρωπον· ἐπειδὴ περὶ μόνα, καθὼς φασιν οἱ τὰ τοιαῦτα δεινοὶ, τὰ ψυχούμενα, καὶ ἁπλῶς τὰ ὑπὸ γένεσιν καὶ φθορὰν, ἡ τῆς φύσεως θεωρεῖσθαι πέφυκεν ἰδιότης.

And the middle, 448being the boundary between these, if it takes man looking to it alone, holds him apart equally from the extremes, neither permitting him to run up to God, and ashamed to let him fall down to the world. As soon, then, as man is moved inwardly by inclination toward any one of these, at once he has exchanged his operation for that very one, and changed his appellation, being called carnal, or psychic, or spiritual. And the work and mark of the carnal is to know only how to do evil; of the psychic, neither ever to wish to do evil nor to suffer it; of the spiritual, to wish only to do good, and, if it befall him to suffer evil for virtue's sake, to accept it eagerly. If, then, thou longest to be led by the Spirit of God, blessed one — as indeed thou dost long — strip from thyself the world and nature; or rather, circumcise thyself from these, and do not refuse to be wronged; do not decline to bear mockeries and insults; and, to speak briefly, suffering evil, never cease to do good to those who do evil, and to remit besides all that is done, for the sake of God and of virtue; according to him who said: If any man will go to law with thee and take thy coat, let him have thy cloak also. Matth. v, 40. And again according to the blessed Apostle, who says: Being reviled, we bless; being persecuted, we bear it; being blasphemed, we entreat. I Cor. iv, 12, 13.

Finally, if thou wouldst be persuaded by me, blessed servant of God, lay up great thanks to those who have afflicted thee; and if thou must be mulcted of everything besides by them, do not refuse; and rather bless when reviled, and bear when persecuted, and entreat when blasphemed; that thou mayest not be carnal, as one who both knows and wishes only to do wrong; nor psychic, as one who does not bear to be wronged; but rather spiritual, as one who both knows and practises willingly only to do good, and knows how to suffer evil eagerly for virtue's sake from those who wish it; looking to the author of our salvation, Jesus, who in return for all the good things that no one has yet been able to grasp by knowledge, endured with long-suffering all the terrible things — such as no one has endured — from sinners and on behalf of sinners. For the aim of the giver of the commandments is to free man from the world and nature. And for this reason he who does not obey is condemned; and in vain do fathers in the world plead their offspring, and the superiors the communities of those who practise the solitary life, as though the commandments must reasonably be idle for them. If we were to accept this as true, the Lord will be shown to have written a law of salvation for no one at all. Now the word of Scripture, as I think, has called the natural man psychic, I Cor. ii, 14. since — as those clever in such things say — the property of nature is wont to be contemplated only in things ensouled, and, simply, in the things subject to coming-to-be and corruption.

Ταῦτα δὲ γέγραφα ὑμῖν, δέσποτα, ἐπειδὴ πολὺ διὰ 449τοῦ ὑμετέρου παραναγνωστικοῦ δυσχεραίνοντας ὑμᾶς εὗρον πρὸς τὰ συμβάντα. Ἀλλ᾿ εὔχου δυνατῶς, ὡς δυνατὸς πρὸς τὸν δυνατὸν ἀφιέναι ἁμαρτίας, ὑπὲρ Μαξίμου τοῦ σοῦ καὶ δούλου καὶ μαθητοῦ, τίμιε Πάτερ.

Ι΄ — Τοῦ αὐτοῦ πρὸς Ἰωάννην κουβικουλάριον. Ἐπειδὴ τὴν αἰτίαν δι᾿ ἣν ὑπ᾿ ἀνθρώπων ἀνθρώπους βασιλεύεσθαι ψηφισάμενος ὁ Θεὸς ἐδικαίωσε, μιᾶς καὶ τῆς αὐτῆς πάντων ὑπαρχούσης τῆς φύσεως, καὶ πάντας τῷ κατ᾿ αὐτὴν ἁπλῷ λόγῳ τοὺς μετέχοντας αὐτῆς ὁμοτίμως ἀποφαινούσης, γραφῆναι ὑμῖν ἐκελεύσατε, ἐρῶ κατὰ τὴν ἐμὴν δύναμιν, μηδὲν ἀποκρυπτόμενος, ὧν παρὰ σοφῶν καὶ μακαρίων ἀνδρῶν παρέλαβον· τὰ πολλὰ περικόπτων ὅσα ῥηθῆναι περὶ τούτου διὰ τὸ σύντομον ἐνδέχεται. Λόγος ἀληθής, ὥς φασι, μυστικῶς ὑπαγορεύει, τὸν δεσπόζειν κατὰ χάριν τοῦ πεποιηκότος αὐτὴν Θεοῦ, παντὸς τοῦ ὁρωμένου κόσμου λαχόντα ἄνθρωπον, διὰ τοῦ κατὰ παράχρησιν τρόπου, τῶν ἐμφύτων δυνάμεων τῆς ἐν αὐτῷ νοερᾶς οὐσίας, πρὸς τὰ παρὰ φύσιν τρέψαντα τὰς κινήσεις, ἑαυτῷ τε καὶ τῷ παντὶ τούτῳ κόσμῳ, τὴν κρατοῦσαν νῦν αὐτῶν ἀλλοίωσιν καὶ φθορὰν, κατὰ κρίσιν εἰσενέγκασθαι τοῦ Θεοῦ δικαίαν· ἵνα μὴ πρὸς τὰ παρὰ φύσιν ἀθάνατος ἐπ᾿ ἄπειρον ἡ τῆς ψυχῆς δύναμις αὐτῷ κινουμένῳ φυλάττοιτο· ὅπερ ἐστὶν οὐ μόνον κακίας τὸ ἔσχατον, καὶ τῆς αὐτοῦ τοῦ ἀνθρώπου ἀληθοῦς ὀντότητος ἀρίδηλος ἔκπτωσις, ἀλλὰ καὶ σαφὴς τῆς θείας ἀγαθότητος ἄρνησις. Διὰ τοῦτο ὁ πολύμοχθος οὗτος, καὶ πολυστένακτος τῶν ἀνθρώπων βίος, διὰ τῆς πολλῆς ἀλογίας καὶ ἀταξίας τῆς αὐτὸν τιθηνούσης ὕλης, ἄλλοτε ἄλλως φέρων τε καὶ φερόμενος· καὶ ἀκριβέστερον εἰπεῖν, ἐν τῷ φέρεσθαι φέρων πάντας ἀνθρώπους, τῶν ἐν αὐτῷ δεινῶν μετόχους πάντας καθίστησι· καὶ οὐδένα παντελῶς ἀφίησι τῆς αὐτοῦ ταραχῆς ἐλεύθερον.

I have written these things to you, master, since I found you, 449through your reader, much vexed at what has befallen. But pray mightily, as one mighty, to him who is mighty to forgive sins, for Maximus, thy servant and disciple, honoured Father.

LETTER 10

Of the same, to John the Cubicularius.

Since you have bidden that there be written to you the cause on account of which God, having decreed it, deemed it just that men be ruled as by kings by men — the nature of all being one and the same, and declaring all who share in it equal in honour by the simple principle according to it — I will speak according to my power, hiding nothing of what I received from wise and blessed men, cutting short for brevity's sake the many things that might be said on this. A true account, as they say, mystically suggests that man — who by the grace of God who made him was allotted the lordship of the whole visible world — having turned, by the mode of misuse, the movements of the innate powers of the intellectual essence in him toward what is contrary to nature, brought upon himself and upon this whole world, by the just judgement of God, the alteration and corruption that now holds sway over them; so that the power of the soul might not be kept immortal to infinity for him while he is moved toward what is contrary to nature; which is not only the extreme of vice, and a manifest falling away from the true being of man himself, but also a clear denial of the divine goodness.

For this reason this life of men, full of toil and full of groaning, through the great irrationality and disorder of the matter that nurses it, carrying and being carried now one way, now another — or, to speak more exactly, in being carried carrying all men — makes all men sharers in the terrible things in it, and leaves no one at all free of its turmoil.

Καὶ τοῦτο κατὰ τὸν πρέποντα τῆς τοῦ Θεοῦ σοφίας διετυπώθη λόγον, ἵνα τὸν παρόντα βίον παραλόγως κατὰ διάθεσιν ὑφ᾿ ἡμῶν ἀγαπώμενον, κἂν ὀψέ ποτε τῷ πάσχειν ὑπ᾿ αὐτοῦ κακῶς κατὰ περίστασιν, ὅσον βλάπτειν οἶδεν ἡ πρὸς αὐτὸν φιλία μεταμαθόντες, γνῶμεν ὅτι πολὺ τῆς πρὸς αὐτὸν ἑνώσεως ὁ χωρισμός ἐστιν ἡμῖν χρησιμώτερος· καὶ τὸ πρὸς αὐτὸν μῖσος, κατὰ λόγον ἐνδίκως κυρώσωμεν· καὶ φυγόντες τὴν τῶν ὁρωμένων φύρσιν καὶ ταραχὴν, πρὸς τὴν τῶν νοουμένων εὐσταθῆ ταυτότητα σωφρόνως τὸν πόθον μεταθώμεθα. 452Ἀλλ᾿ ἐπειδὴ καὶ μαστιζόμενοι, καὶ μυρία πάσχοντες δεινὰ, τῆς πρὸς αὐτὸν φιλίας τὸν δεσμὸν λύειν οὐκ ἀνεχόμεθα, κατὰ πρόνοιαν ὡς σοφὸς καὶ ἀγαθὸς, τὸν τῆς βασιλείας τοῖς ἀνθρώποις ὁ Θεὸς διετύπωσε νόμον· τὴν προγνωσθεῖσαν διὰ τὴν ἄνεσιν ἔσεσθαι κατὰ τὸν βίον πολλὴν τῆς κακίας λύσσαν ἀναστέλλων πόρρωθεν· ἵνα μὴ γένωνται κατὰ τοὺς ἰχθύας τῆς θαλάσσης, ἀλλήλων οἱ ἄνθρωποι παραναλώματα· μηδενὸς ἐπιστατοῦντος, καὶ τὴν ἄδικον τοῦ δυνατωτέρου κατὰ τοῦ ὑποβεβηκότος ὁρμὴν κωλύοντος. Διὰ τοῦτο, κατὰ τὸ εἰκὸς, ὡς οἶμαι, βασιλεία τῶν ἀνθρώπων τῷ γένει ἀναγκαίως ἐπεισήχθη· σοφίαν παρὰ Θεοῦ λαβοῦσα, καὶ δυναστείαν· καὶ ἡ ὁμότιμος φύσις εἰς τὸ ἄρχειν καὶ ἄρχεσθαι μερισθῆναι συνεχωρήθη. Ὅπως τῇ μὲν, τάσσῃ τοὺς νομίμως τοῖς θεσμοῖς τῆς φύσεως πειθομένοις· τῇ δὲ, κολάζῃ δικαίως τοὺς εἴκειν τούτοις δι᾿ αὐθάδειαν γνώμης μὴ βουλομένους· καὶ τὴν δικαιοσύνην ἐντεῦθεν πᾶσι χαρίζηται, πόθῳ καὶ φόβῳ βραβευομένην· δι᾿ ἧς τὸ ἀνώμαλον τῆς τοῦ καθ᾿ ἕκαστον γνώμης ἀπογενόμενον, τὸ ἐν πᾶσι τῆς φύσεως ἴσον γαληνόν τε καὶ ἥμερον λαμπρῶς διαδείκνυται. Ὅπερ οὐκ ἂν ἐγεγόνει ποτὲ, μὴ τοῦ φόβου κολάζοντος τὴν ἄφρονα τῶν πολλῶν πρὸς ἀτοπίαν ὁρμὴν, καὶ βίᾳ πρὸς τῶν σωφρόνων δι᾿ ἀπειλῆς ἄγοντος γαληνότητα. Ὃ γὰρ πέφυκε λόγος τοὺς σώφρονας πείθειν ἑκουσίως ἀσπάζεσθαι, τοῦτο στέργειν ὁ φόβος τοὺς ἄφρονας εἴωθεν ἀκουσίως βιάζεσθαι. Διὰ ταύτην ἐγὼ τὴν αἰτίαν τῷ βίῳ τῶν ἀνθρώπων παρεισελθεῖν τὴν βασιλείαν συγχωρηθῆναι μεμάθηκα. καὶ ἀποδέχομαι τὸν λόγον ὡς ἀληθῆ, καὶ τῆς αἰτίας διδάσκαλον. Εἰ δέ τις καὶ ἕτερος ταύτης λόγος καθέστηκεν αἴτιος κατὰ τὴν θείαν Γραφὴν, τοῦτον εἶναι μόνοις ἡγοῦμαι ληπτὸν τοῖς καθαροῖς τὴν διάνοιαν. Πλὴν δ᾿ ὅμως, οὐδ᾿ οὗτος τοῦ σκοποῦ τῆς θείας ὁλοσχερῶς ἀπάδει Γραφῆς. Κἀκείνη γὰρ παραιτησαμένοις ἀνθρώποις τὴν τοῦ Θεοῦ βασιλείαν, συγχωροῦντα τὸν Θεὸν ἀφηγεῖται, κἂν ὑφ᾿ ἑαυτῶν βασιλεύεσθαι· ἵνα μὴ τὸ ἄτακτον τῆς ἀναρχίας πολυαρχίαν ἐργάσηται καὶ στάσιν φθοροποιὸν ἐντεῦθεν τῷ παντὶ γένει τῶν ἀνθρώπων ἐνεργάσηται, μηδενὸς τὴν αὐτῶν ἐπιμέλειαν κατὰ θείαν ψῆφον πεπιστευομένου· καὶ λόγῳ μὲν τάσσοντος τοὺς διὰ λόγου παιδαγωγουμένους πρὸς ἡμερότητα· δυναστείας δὲ φόβῳ κολάζοντος, τοὺς τὴν πονηρίαν τεκταίνοντας· καὶ τὸ ἔμφυτον τῆς γνώσεως ἀγαθὸν γνωμικῶς τοῖς παρὰ φύσιν τρόποις ἐφ᾿ ἑαυτῶν διαφθείροντας. Ὅστις οὖν βασιλέων, τοῦτον οὕτω φυλάξαι τὸν νόμον τῆς βασιλείας ἐσπούδασι, Θεοῦ δεύτερος ἐπὶ γῆς ἀληθῶς ἀναπέφανται, ὡς θείας βουλῆς πιστοτάτας [word?] ὁμενος ὑπουργὸς, καὶ τὸ βασιλεύεσθαι ὑπὸ Θεοῦ, βασιλεύειν ἀνθρώπων δικαίως λαχών.

And this was ordained according to the principle befitting the wisdom of God, so that, as to the present life, unreasonably loved by us in disposition, having learned at last, even late, through suffering evil from it by circumstance, how much the friendship for it knows how to harm, we may know that separation from it is far more useful to us than union with it; and may justly ratify, according to reason, our hatred of it; and, fleeing the commixture and turmoil of the things seen, may soberly transfer our longing to the steadfast identity of the things understood.

452But since, even when scourged, and suffering countless terrible things, we do not endure to loose the bond of friendship for it, God, as wise and good, according to providence ordained for men the law of kingship; checking from afar the great frenzy of vice that he foreknew would arise in life through licence; so that men might not become, like the fish of the sea, the prey of one another, with no one presiding and hindering the unjust impulse of the stronger against the one beneath him. For this reason, as is likely, I think, kingship was of necessity introduced to the race of men, receiving wisdom from God, and might; and the nature equal in honour was permitted to be divided into ruling and being ruled. So that by the one it might marshal those who lawfully obey the ordinances of nature, and by the other justly punish those who are unwilling to yield to these through self-will of inclination; and might thence bestow on all justice, governed by longing and fear; through which, the unevenness of each man's inclination being removed, the equality of nature in all, calm and gentle, is brilliantly displayed. Which would never have come to pass, had not fear punished the senseless impulse of the many toward absurdity, and led them by force through threat toward the calm of the sober. For what reason is of a nature to persuade the sober to embrace willingly, that fear is wont to force the senseless to accept unwillingly.

For this cause I have learned that kingship was permitted to enter the life of men; and I accept the account as true, and as a teacher of the cause. But if there is also some other account standing as cause of it according to divine Scripture, I think it is graspable only by those pure in understanding. Yet even this one does not wholly disagree with the aim of divine Scripture. For it too narrates that God permitted men who had refused the kingship of God to be ruled at least by themselves; so that the disorder of anarchy might not work a rule of many, and thence produce a destructive faction for the whole race of men, there being no one entrusted by divine decree with the care of them — marshalling by reason those who are trained by reason toward gentleness, and punishing by fear of might those who devise wickedness, and who by inclination corrupt in themselves, through modes contrary to nature, the innate good of knowledge.

Whoever of kings, then, has striven to keep this law of kingship so, has truly been shown forth a second to God upon earth, as having become a most faithful minister of the divine counsel, and as having justly obtained, by being ruled by God, to rule over men.

Ὅστις δὲ τοῦτον παρωσάμενος τὸν θεσμὸν, ἑαυτῷ βασιλεύειν πάντως διέγνω, καὶ οὐ Θεῷ, τἀναντία τούτοις εἰκότως ἐργάσηται· τυραννικῶς μὲν ἑαυτοῦ τοὺς 453ἀγαθοὺς ἐκτινάσσων, καὶ πάσης πόρῥω τιθέμενος βουλῆς καὶ δυναστείας· νεωτερικῶς δὲ τοὺς πονηροὺς ἐπισπώμενος, καὶ πάσης αὐτοὺς κυρίους τῆς αὐτοῦ καθιστῶν ἐξουσίας· ὅπερ ἐστὶ τῶν κρατούντων τε καὶ κρατουμένων ἔσχατον ἀπωλείας βάραθρον. Ἡμῖν δὲ δοίη Θεὸς ἑκουσίως ὑπ᾿ αὐτοῦ βασιλεύεσθαι, διὰ τῆς ἐκπληρώσεως τῶν ζωοποιῶν αὐτοῦ ἐντολῶν· καὶ τοὺς κατ᾿ αὐτὸν ἐπὶ γῆς βασιλεύοντας, ἀξίως τιμᾶν, ὡς φύλακας τῶν αὐτοῦ θείων διαταγμάτων.

ΙΑ΄.— Τοῦ αὐτοῦ πρὸς ἡγουμένην Videtur hæc, quam Photius Ἰανίαν appellat., περὶ ἀσκητρίας ἐξελθούσης τῆς. μονῆς, καὶ μετανοησάσης. Οἱ τῆς ἀληθείας κήρυκες, καὶ τῆς θείας χάριτος γενόμενοι λειτουργοί, ὅσοι ἐξ ἀρχῆς μέχρις ἡμῶν κατὰ τοὺς ἑαυτῶν καιροὺς ἕκαστος τὸ σωτήριον ἡμῖν τοῦ Θεοῦ διεσάφησαν θέλημα, φασὶ, μηδὲν οὕτω θεῷ εἶναι φίλον τε καὶ ἐράσμιον, ὡς ἡ ἐκ μετανοίας ἀληθοῦς ἐπ᾿ αὐτὸν τῶν ἀνθρώπων ἐπιστροφή. Ὅπερ μάλιστα τῶν ἄλλων θειότερον ὑπάρχον δεῖξαι βουλόμενος ὁ θεαρχικὸς τοῦ Θεοῦ καὶ Πατρὸς Λόγος· μᾶλλον δὲ καὶ πρῶτον καὶ μόνον τῆς ἀπείρου ἀγαθότητος γνώρισμα, κατά τινα ἄῤῥητον συγκαταβάσεως τρόπον, διὰ σαρκὸς ἡμῖν ὁμιλῆσαι ἠξίωσεν, ἐκεῖνα καὶ δράσας καὶ παθὼν καὶ εἰπών, δι᾿ ὧν ἔδει ἡμᾶς, ἐχθροὺς ὄντας καὶ πολεμίους καταλλαγῆναι τῷ Θεῷ καὶ Πατρὶ, καὶ τῆς μακαρίας ζωῆς ξενωθέντας, πρὸς αὐτὴν πάλιν ἐπαναχθῆναι. Οὐ μόνον γὰρ τοῖς θαύμασι τὰς νόσους ἡμῶν ἰάσατο, καὶ διὰ παθημάτων τὰς ἀσθενείας ἀναλαβὼν, καὶ διὰ θανάτου ἀποδοὺς ὡς ὑπεύθυνος ὁ ἀναμάρτητος τὸ ἡμέτερον ὄφλημα τῶν πολλῶν καὶ φοβερῶν ἡμᾶς ἠλευθέρωσεν ἐγκλημάτων· ἀλλὰ καὶ πολυτρόπως διδάσκων, ἴσους ἑαυτῷ γενέσθαι κατὰ τὴν φιλάνθρωπον ἕξιν, καὶ τὴν εἰς ἀλλήλους τελείαν ἀγάπην, βουλομένους βεβούληται. Διά τοι τοῦτο, Οὐκ ἦλθον καλέσαι δικαίους, ἐβόα, ἀλλὰ ἁμαρτωλοὺς εἰς μετάνοιαν. Καὶ, Οὐ χρείαν ἔχουσιν οἱ ἰσχύοντες ἰατροῦ, ἀλλ᾿ οἱ κακῶς ἔχοντες. Καὶ, Ζητῆσαι, καὶ σῶσαι τὸ ἀπολωλὸς, ἐληλυθέναι. Καὶ, Πρὸς τὰ πρόβατα, τὰ ἀπολωλότα οἴκου Ἰσραὴλ ἀπεστάλθαι. Καὶ τὴν βασιλικὴν ἀνασώσασθαι εἰκόνα, ὑπὸ τὴν δυσωδεστάτην τῶν παθῶν χόπρον γεγενημένην παραγεγονέναι, διὰ τῆς δραχμῆς παραβολικῶς παρηνίξατο. Καὶ, Ἀμὴν λέγω ὑμῖν ὅτι χαρὰ γίνεται ἐν οὐρανῷ καὶ ἐπὶ γῆς, ἐπὶ ἑνὶ ἁμαρτωλῷ μετανοοῦντι. Διὰ τοῦτο τὸν λῃσταῖς περιπεσόντα, καὶ γυμνωθέντα πάσης περιβολῆς, καὶ ταῖς πληγαῖς ἡμιθανῆ γεγενημένον, οἴνῳ καὶ ἐλαίῳ καὶ καταδέσμοις ἀνέῤῥωσε· καὶ ἐπὶ τὸ ἴδιον κτῆνος ἀναβιβάσας, τῷ πανδοχεῖ παρατίθεται· καὶ τὰ πρὸς ἐπιμέλειαν ἀρκοῦντα δοὺς, ἐπηγγείλατο καὶ τὰ προσδαπανώμενα δώσειν ἐπανερχόμενος.

But whoever, thrusting aside this ordinance, has resolved to reign altogether for himself, and not for God, will fittingly work the contrary of these things: tyrannically shaking off from himself 453the good, and putting them far from all counsel and might; and with innovating rashness drawing in the wicked, and making them lords of all his authority; which is the extreme abyss of destruction both for those who hold power and for those held under it. But may God grant us to be ruled willingly by him, through the fulfilment of his life-giving commandments; and to honour worthily those who reign on earth according to him, as guardians of his divine ordinances.

LETTER 11

Of the same, to an abbess, concerning a nun who had left the monastery, and had repented.

The heralds of the truth, and those who became ministers of the divine grace — all who from the beginning up to us, each in his own times, have made plain to us the saving will of God — say that nothing is so dear and lovely to God as the turning of men to him out of true repentance. Wishing to show this as above all others the more divine thing, the thearchic Word of God and the Father — or rather, the first and only mark of the infinite goodness — deigned, according to a certain ineffable mode of condescension, to converse with us through flesh, both doing and suffering and saying those things through which we, being enemies and foes, had to be reconciled to God and the Father, and, having been estranged from the blessed life, to be led back again to it. For not only did he heal our diseases by his wonders, and, taking up our infirmities through sufferings, and through death paying our debt as though liable — he the sinless — free us from our many and fearful charges; but also, teaching in many ways, he has wished that we should, by our own wish, become equal to himself according to the habit of love for man, and in perfect love toward one another.

For this very reason he cried: I came not to call the righteous, but sinners to repentance. Matth. ix, 13. And: They that are strong need not a physician, but they that are sick. ibid. 12. And that he had come to seek and to save that which was lost. And that he had been sent to the sheep that are lost of the house of Israel. And through the drachma he hinted in a parable that he had come to recover the royal image that had fallen under the most foul-smelling dung of the passions. Luc. xv, 8 seqq. And: Amen I say to you that there is joy in heaven and on earth over one sinner that repenteth. ibid. 7. For this reason he restored, with wine and oil and bandages, him who fell among robbers and was stripped of all clothing and left half dead by his wounds; and having set him upon his own beast, he commits him to the innkeeper; and having given what sufficed for his care, he promised to give on his return what should be spent besides. Luc. x, 30 seqq.

Διὰ τοῦτο πρὸς τὴν ἐπιστροφὴν τοῦ ἀσώτου υἱοῦ τὸν πανάγαθον πατέρα ἐπικλιθῆναι φησι, καὶ ἀσπασάμενον 456τὸν πρὸς αὐτὸν διὰ τῆς μετανοίας ἀναδραμόντα, τῷ κάλλει τῆς πατρικῆς πάλιν κατακοσμῆσαι δόξης, οὐδοτιοῦν περὶ τῶν προτέρων μεμψάμενον. Διὰ τοῦτο τὸ τῆς θείας ἑκατοντάδος ἀποφοιτῆσαν πρόβατον, ἐπὶ τὰ ὄρη καὶ τοὺς βουνοὺς πλανώμενον εὑρών, οὐκ ἐλαύνων, οὐδὲ μόχθῳ κατατρίβων πρὸς τὴν μάνδραν ἐπανήγαγε τῶν προβάτων· ἀλλὰ τῶν ὤμων ἐπιθεὶς τῶν ἰδίων, πρὸς τὰ σύννομα συμπαθῶς διεσώσατο. Διὰ τοῦτο, Δεῦτε πρός με, πάντες οἱ κοπιῶντες καὶ πεφορτισμένοι τῇ καρδίᾳ, κἀγὼ ἀναπαύσω ὑμᾶς, ἀνέκραγε. Καὶ, Ἄρατε τὸν ζυγόν μου ἐφ᾿ ὑμᾶς· ζυγὸν λέγων, τὰς ἐντολάς· ἤγουν τὸν εὐαγγελικῶς εὐθυνόμενον βίον· φορτίον δὲ, τὸ δοκοῦν εἶναι τῆς μετανοίας ἐπίπονον· Ὁ γὰρ ζυγός μου, φησὶ, χρηστὸς καὶ τὸ φορτίον μου ἐλαφρόν. Καὶ πάλιν θείαν δικαιοσύνην καὶ ἀγαθότητα διδάσκων, παρακελεύεται λέγων· Γίνεσθε ἅγιοι· γίνεσθε τέλειοι· γίνεσθε ἐλεήμονες· γίνεσθε οἰκτίρμονες, ὡς ὁ Πατὴρ ὑμῶν ὁ οὐράνιος. Καὶ, Ἄφετε, καὶ ἀφεθήσεται ὑμῖν. Καὶ, Ὅσα θέλετε ἵνα ποιῶσιν ὑμῖν οἱ ἄνθρωποι, ὁμοίως καὶ ὑμεῖς ποιεῖτε αὐτοῖς. Καὶ τὰ τοιαῦτα. Καὶ ἁπλῶς, ἵνα μὴ τὰ καθ᾿ ἕκαστον λέγων μηκύνω τὴν ἐπιστολὴν, διὰ τοῦτο πόρνας δικαιοῖ μετανοούσας, τελώνας τε προσδέχεται ἐπιστρέφοντας, καὶ τοῦ Εὐαγγελίου καθίστησι κήρυκας. Διὰ τοῦτο ληστὰς ὁμολογοῦντας, καὶ τῷ θανάτῳ ὠνουμένους τὴν σωτηρίαν, διὰ τὸν φόβον συνεπάγεται Alter Reg. cod. συνεπάγεται., καὶ εἰς τὸν παράδεισον ἑαυτῷ συνεισάγει, τῶν μελλόντων ἀγαθῶν πιστούμενος τὰς ἐπαγγελίας. Διὰ τοῦτο διώκτας μαινομένους, σωφρονίζει, διὰ τὸν ζηλον, καὶ ἐθνῶν ἀποστόλους ἀνθ᾿ ἑαυτοῦ χειροτονήσας, προβάλλεται· ἵνα ἡμεῖς μάθωμεν φιλανθρωπίαν καὶ ἡμερότητα· καὶ μήτε ῥαδίως ἑαυτῶν ἀπογινώσκωμεν ἁμαρτάνοντες, καὶ πρὸς τοὺς ἁμαρτάνοντας μὴ μένωμεν ἀσυμπαθεῖς καὶ ἀνάλγητοι· Θεὸν ἔχοντες τῶν γινομένων παράδειγμα. Ἐπειδὴ οὖν ταῦθ᾿ οὕτως ἐπ᾿ αὐτῆς ἔχειν τῆς ἀληθείας, καὶ θέλημα Θεοῦ ὑπάρχειν τὸ πάντας ἀνθρώπους σωθῆναι, καὶ εἰς ἐπίγνωσιν ἐλθεῖν ἀληθείας, ἐδιδάχθημεν· καὶ τῆς πρὸς ἡμᾶς αὐτοῦ θαυμαστῆς διὰ σαρκὸς ἐπιδημίας ἀρχὴν καὶ ὑπόθεσιν μόνην τὴν ἡμῶν σωτηρίαν εἶναι μεμνήμεθα· θαρρῶν αἰτῶ τὴν ὑμετέραν ἁγιωσύνην, αὐτά σοι τὰ ζωοποιὰ παθήματα τοῦ Θεοῦ καὶ Σωτῆρος ἡμῶν Χριστοῦ, οἷς ἐλυτρώθημεν τῆς ἐξουσίας τοῦ σκότους, ἀνθ᾿ ἱκετηρίας προβαλλόμενος.

For this reason he says that the all-good father inclined Luc. xv, 20 seqq. toward the return of the prodigal son, and having embraced 456him who ran back to him through repentance, adorned him again with the beauty of the father's glory, reproaching him in nothing whatever about what had gone before. For this reason, having found the sheep that had strayed from the divine hundred wandering on the mountains and hills, he did not drive it, nor wear it out with toil, as he brought it back to the fold of the sheep; but laying it on his own shoulders, he restored it in sympathy safe to its fellows of the pasture. ibid. 4-7. For this reason he cried aloud: Come unto me, all ye that labour and are heavy laden in heart, and I will give you rest. And: Take my yoke upon you — calling the commandments a yoke, that is, the life directed according to the Gospel; and a burden, what seems to be the toilsomeness of repentance: For my yoke, he says, is kind, and my burden light. Matth. xi, 28. And again, teaching divine justice and goodness, he exhorts, saying: Be holy; be perfect; be merciful; be compassionate, as your heavenly Father. Levit. xi, 44. And: Forgive, and it shall be forgiven you. And: Whatsoever ye would that men should do to you, do ye also to them likewise. Matth. vii, 12. And such things. And, simply, not to lengthen the letter by telling each thing, for this reason he justifies harlots who repent, and receives publicans who turn, and makes them heralds of the Gospel. For this reason he takes along with him robbers who confess, and who buy salvation with death, on account of their fear, and brings them in with himself into paradise, Luc. xxiii, 43. confirming the promises of the good things to come. For this reason he brings persecutors raging in their zeal to soberness, and, having ordained them apostles of the gentiles in his own stead, puts them forward; Act. ix, 1 seqq. so that we may learn love for man and gentleness; and may neither readily despair of ourselves when we sin, nor remain unsympathetic and unfeeling toward those who sin, having God as the pattern of what is done.

Since, then, we have been taught that these things are so in very truth, and that it is the will of God that all men be saved and come to the knowledge of the truth; I Tim. ii, 4. and we remember that the beginning and the only purpose of his wondrous sojourn with us through the flesh is our salvation: I take courage and ask your holiness, putting before thee as a suppliant's branch the very life-giving sufferings of Christ our God and Saviour, by which we were ransomed from the power of darkness.

Πιστεύω γάρ σε γνησίαν οὖσαν μαθῆτιν τοῦ Υἱοῦ τοῦ Θεοῦ, διὰ πάσης τῆς τοῦ βίου ἀγωγῆς, ζῶντα καὶ ἔμπρακτον ἐν ἑαυτῇ φέρειν τὸν διδάσκαλον, ἅτε δὴ ἐκ παιδὸς δι᾿ ὅλου αὐτῷ προσχωρήσασαν· ἵνα καὶ δι᾿ ἐμοῦ μεσίτου τοῦ ταπεινοῦ 457καὶ ἁμαρτωλοῦ, καὶ μηδὲν ἔχοντος τῆς σῆς ἀρετῆς ἐπάξιον, τιμήσῃς τὴν ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν αὐτοῦ φιλάνθρωπον κένωσιν, καὶ προσδέξῃ μετανοοῦσαν, καὶ τοῖς δάκρυσιν ἐκτακεῖσαν τήνδε τὴν ἀδελφήν. Πολλὴ γάρ ἐστὶ καὶ ἄφατος ἡ συντριβὴ τῆς καρδίας αὐτῆς, καὶ τοῦ πνεύματος ἡ ταπείνωσις, ἐπί τε τῇ ἁμαρτίᾳ ᾗ πέπραχε, καὶ τῷ ἀφ᾿ ὑμῶν χωρισμῷ, ὃν πέπονθεν. Μὴ οὖν ἐξουδενώσῃς δούλη Θεοῦ εὐλογημένη, καρδίαν συντετριμμένην καὶ τεταπεινωμένην, ἣν ὁ Θεὸς οὐκ ἐξουδένωσεν· ἀλλ᾿ οἰκτίρμων καὶ ἐλεήμων, εὔσπλαγχνός τε καὶ συμπαθὴς γενομένη, μίμησαι τὸν δι᾿ ἡμᾶς ἑκουσίως ὑπὲρ φιλανθρωπίας ἀποθανεῖν καταδεξάμενον· καὶ προσδέξασθαι αὐτὴν καὶ ἀσπάσασθαι ἀξίωσον, καὶ κυρῶσαι πρὸς αὐτὴν ἀγάπην ἀνάσχου. Καὶ στήριξον αὐτὴν λόγοις καὶ ἔργοις φιλανθρωπίας καὶ ἡμερότητος, καὶ ἅρπασον τῶν χειρῶν τοῦ τυράννου, καὶ τὰ γενόμενα αὐτῇ συντρίμματα κατάδησον συμπαθῶς· ἵνα μὴ πολὺ καταυχρηθεῖσα τῇ λύπῃ, τῷ τῆς ἀπογνώσεως ἐμπαρῇ σκόλοπι· καὶ ἀπαιτηθῶμεν αἷμα ψυχῆς ἐξ ἡμῶν παραχθείσης· ἢ τό γε ἀληθέστερον εἰπεῖν, προσδοθείσης τῷ ἐχθρῷ τῆς ζωῆς ἡμῶν, πρὸς τὸ ἔχειν ὑφήνιον εἰς ὅπερ ἂν καὶ βούλεται· ἣν ἔδει ζῆν διὰ τῆς ἡμῶν μακροθυμίας καὶ ἡπιότητος· καὶ ἐγκληθῶμεν δικαίως, ὡς τῶν θείων ἀμελήσαντες καὶ θελημάτων καὶ παθημάτων. Ὁμοίως γάρ, κἂν εἰ σκληρὸς ὁ λόγος, ἀλλ᾿ οὖν ἀληθής, τὸ ἀγαθὸν τοῦ Θεοῦ ἀτιμάζουσι θέλημα, καὶ τὴν ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν αὐτοῦ φιλάνθρωπον κένωσιν ἐξουδενοῦσιν, ὅσον τὸ ἐπ᾿ αὐτοῖς, οἵ τε μετανοεῖν οὐκ ἐθέλοντες, καὶ τοὺς μετανοοῦντας μὴ προσδεχόμενοι· ὅτι ἀλλήλων ὀφείλοντες οἰκειοῦσθαι τὰς συμφορὰς, διὰ τὴν ὁμοτιμίαν τῆς φύσεως· οὐδ᾿ αὐτὴν τὴν περὶ ἡμᾶς τοῦ Θεοῦ ἀγαθότητα αἰδεσθέντες· εἰ μή τι ἄλλο περὶ τὸ ὁμόφυλον μιμησάμενοι, διεδείχθημεν.
